# Review handoff — f-20261017-04 plan-only run (2026-10-31)

Plan: `tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md`. BASE `385287ef6eedc3a88a2cd5875cb663d3ad0a4dd6`. Drain session `ed1f8c53-105f-4b67-957f-377a0b029243`, executor `claude`. Plan authorship and arbitration shared one context (Claude Opus orchestrator). The lenses ran as Claude CLI read-only leaves in throwaway worktrees, the same Anthropic model family as the orchestrator, so family separation was absent. No implementation code exists yet.

## Outcome

Plan review converged after 6 rounds, plus repair closure round r7 over the carried-list edit (supervisor reason `plan-changed-after-review`). Every lens's latest verdict is APPROVED, and no plan-level issue is open. CR-1 to CR-6 are carried to cumulative diff review and must be copied verbatim into the phase brief and every step-6 packet. Entry revalidation kept `build`: the finding's open question (where eviction lives and what it keeps) was answered by this plan, not by a prior decision. No decision in `tasks/decisions.md` names this finding.

## Witness parsing note

`plan-meta.json` carries the 33 issues whose raw lens lines the supervisor's finding parser recognises (`^\s*\[kind\]`). The others were reported with a leading `- `, a numeral or a backtick. Their claims, witnesses, dispositions and closures are recorded below and in the plan's `## Reviews`, which is the complete history. Every parsed finding is witnessed by an issue in `plan-meta.json`.

## Orchestrator probes (disposable crate under RUN_TMP `cargo-lock-probe`, outside every checkout)

- **Locks during compile:** with a `build.rs` sleeping 25 s, `flock -n -E 75` exited 75 on `.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock` and `.cargo-artifact-lock`, and 0 after the build.
- **E1, locks during tests:** while a 15 s test binary ran under `cargo test`, all three exited 0.
- **E2, fresh runs:** a fully fresh `cargo build` and `cargo test`, with the stamp file outside the package, wrote nothing in `target/debug`.
- **E5:** of 91 same-user processes, 7 have an EACCES `/proc/<pid>/exe` (systemd, sd-pam, kwin_wayland, polkit-kde-auth, org_kde_powerde, keepassxc, gpg-agent).
- **Machine state at BASE:** sizes in the plan's Traced premises. `/` is mounted `noatime`. All four side-lane branches are merged and clean.

## Decided autonomously (for the adopter to record through `record-decision`)

The plan's `## Decisions and trade-offs` holds each choice, its rejected alternatives and its reason:

- **Where:** push-gate runner P0 advisory step, not a timer, drain, pre-review or agent-kit.
- **What it keeps:** a 40 GiB per-root cap in the gating checkout; a 7-day idle rule elsewhere; no eviction while a unit is in use.
- **Shared target directory:** rejected.
- **Worktree half:** belongs to agent-kit's existing `kit worktree remove`, so no change and no filing.
- **Eviction style:** lock-held, in place.

Reversal path: delete the P0 entry and the script. Nothing else depends on them.

## Open obligation after implementation

The finding's one-week `du -sh` observation cannot complete within the run. Follow next-finding §5 "Exit with an external wait" if it is still pending at release.

Lenses (Claude executor, read-only leaves): review-plan REVISE · review-minimalism REVISE ·

`tasks/plans/` copy instead (byte-identical at r1).

Evidence gathered during triage (disposable probes under `$RUN_TMP/cargo-lock-probe`):

* **E1:** during `cargo test` of a crate whose test sleeps 15 s, while the test binary ran,
  `flock -n -E 75` exited 0 on `.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock` and `.cargo-artifact-lock`.
  Cargo frees its build locks while tests run.
* **E2:** a fully fresh `cargo build` and `cargo test` (stamp file outside the package)
  wrote nothing in `target/debug`. Only interleaved `cargo clippy` recompiled and wrote.
  A gate with no compile leaves the idle signal unchanged.
* **E3:** `~/.claude/references/gate-performance.md:40-45`: `agent-gate` admits two
  machine-wide slots, so two checkouts can gate concurrently.
* **E4:** `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` `runSerialStep` results feed `firstFailure`
  (`:990`), which sets the exit code, and P0 entries are read as `p0[0]` / `p0[1]`
  (`:1144`, `:1157`). `PRE_REVIEW_GATE_SCHEDULE` has no `p0`.

### Round 5 (revision r5, 2026-10-31, wall 202 s for the lens group)

Closure lenses: review-plan, review-tests, review-correctness, review-error-handling, all

Closed in round 5: I55, I60, I61, I62, I63, I65, I66, I67. I39 reported NOT CLOSED, its second
failed closure.

**I39 lineage judgment (second failed closure, build §4 escalation):** the invariant is
that Risks must not claim a trip rate the evidence does not support. The previous answer
derived a rate from the finding's daily figures, then from two rounded sizes of different
checkouts. New evidence (review-plan r5): the daily figures are days 3 and 4 of a worktree,
not its initial build, and growth follows compile count (Traced premises: each compile
adds an incremental session). Judgment: the simplest correct statement claims no rate. It
says the rate follows compiles, the one-week observation measures it, and a trip more often
than weekly reopens the cap. Changed: the Risks bullet. The focused fresh-context review-plan
check of this judgment runs in round 6.

      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O3/O4 (`2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:103-118`) — O3 promises eviction \"never fails a gate\", but O4 wires it as a P0 step through `runSerialStep`. In `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:1050-1056` every step lands in `stepResults`, and `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:1290` and `:1132` turn the first `failed` or `interrupted` step into the run's exit code. An uncaught throw, an OOM kill, SIGTERM or a missing package script would make `rust-target-eviction` fail, and the gate would go red over housekeeping. \"Exits 0 unless its invocation is malformed\" covers only per-unit errors, not the process failing. The plan has no top-level catch-all that forces exit 0 and no advisory-step semantics in the runner. It also doesn't say that a failed or interrupted eviction step must not skip lanes, as `setup-rust` and the mutation guard do. O6 has no runner-level test where the step exits non-zero and the gates stay green. (confidence: 88)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O3 (`:105-109`) — Eviction failures are invisible when the run passes. Per-unit lines go to the step's log file. `printSummary` (`scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:948-988`) prints a status row for each step, but only failed or interrupted steps get a log tail. So \"failed with error\" and \"skipped: lock held\" produce no output the user or drain sees. A permanently failing unit, for example root-owned files from a container build or a stuck lock, would let target directories grow again with no signal until the one-week `du` check. The plan should specify a one-line warning in the runner output, or a non-green status such as \"passed (warnings)\", whenever any unit failed. It should also say what happens when the same unit is skipped on every run. (confidence: 85)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O2 (`:92-101`) — The lifetime of the process that holds the `flock` is not specified. Node can't hold a lock itself, so it has to use a `flock` child. If that child outlives a killed eviction process (SIGKILL, scope teardown, runner interrupt), it keeps the lock on `.cargo-lock` and every later cargo build in that checkout blocks silently. That matches the async-resource rule: every spawn needs an owner and cleanup on every exit path. The plan should say the removal runs inside the lock holder, so the lock dies with it. For example, `flock -n <file> -c <remove>`, or children that are killed on exit and on signals. It should also add a test where the evictor is killed mid-removal and the lock is released. (confidence: 82)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O2/O1 (`:78-101`) — Fail direction on ambiguous inputs is not stated, and for a deletion it must be \"skip and report\". Four cases are open:"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "plan O2 (`:100-101`) — \"A partly removed unit is still a valid, smaller cargo directory … needs no recovery step\" is asserted, not traced. If `.fingerprint` survives while `deps/*` or `build/*/output` are removed, cargo's freshness check may not detect every missing output. That would give a build failure or stale artifact, which the gate would then report as a Rust failure. The plan should fix a removal order that never leaves a fingerprint without its outputs, for example `.fingerprint` first. It should also add an interrupted-eviction test that runs a real `cargo check` on a tiny crate. Until then, treat this as a named limitation: I couldn't trace cargo's internals. (confidence: 78 as a defect, reported because it underpins the \"no recovery step\" claim)"

    "artefact": "lens-platform-semantics-r1.jsonl",
    "findings": [
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:108 — The \"non-Linux exits 0\" claim has no stated detection rule and no proof. \"A host without Linux `flock`\" could mean probing for the binary, and macOS can have a Homebrew `flock` whose flags (`-n`, `-E`) differ from util-linux. A probe-by-binary could then run eviction on macOS with different lock semantics. Gate on `process.platform === \"linux\"` first, as `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs:421` does, and make the platform injectable so the O6 test can exercise the non-Linux branch. The plan should also say that the ubuntu `test` job is the only place these tests run, so the macOS and Windows no-op path is proven by injection and not by CI. (confidence: 50)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:92 — The plan doesn't say how a `flock` is held while the Node process deletes entries. Node has no `flock`, so the only option is a `flock(1)` child holding each lock. If the gate runner is killed (timeout, SIGKILL, closed terminal), an orphaned holder keeps the `.cargo-lock` inodes locked. Every later cargo in that checkout would then block indefinitely. Linux has no default parent-death signal, and other Unixes differ again. The plan should require the holder to exit when its stdin pipe closes, or to run under a bounded lifetime. O6 should test that killing the parent releases the lock. (confidence: 55)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:80 — The oversize predicate uses \"apparent size\", but the 32 GiB threshold is calibrated against `du` figures (17 GB for `debug`). `du` counts each hard-linked inode once. A Node walk that sums `st_size` per path counts hard links repeatedly. Cargo's incremental session directories and its `deps`-to-`debug/` artifact links use hard links. The plan should dedupe by `dev:ino` (`nlink > 1`) and say so. Without that, the threshold is not comparable to the measurement it cites. A fresh `debug` could land near 32 GiB and be evicted every run, which contradicts \"a normally warm unit never trips it\". (confidence: 55)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "nit",
        "text": "tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:78 — The idle signal assumes \"every compile\" updates the unit directory and its direct `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental` children, but the probe only shows that for an actual compile. A fully fresh cargo run (no source change, so gates are warm) does not create new entries there. Cargo only touches `invoked.timestamp` under `-Zmtime-on-use`, and that file is a grandchild anyway. A checkout that is gated daily without source changes, such as main after a no-op pull, would still be evicted after 7 days. The plan should either measure a no-op `cargo clippy` and `cargo test` and confirm what is written, or accept that case. The bound itself is unaffected. (confidence: 40)"
      }
    ],

        "text": "r4 body:104-107 / 221-223 — The idle rule now says an absent `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` or `incremental` child \"is not an error\". O6 has no case for it. The idle-rule cases only vary which present child is fresh. `target/release` units normally have no `incremental` directory. An implementation that treats ENOENT there as undetermined passes every listed test, yet it would report exit 1 on every gate and never evict release units. Add a fixture that omits `incremental` (and one that omits another child). It must be evictable when backdated and kept when fresh. (confidence: 85)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "r4 body:234-237 / 88-89, 152-153 — The delta rewrote the `/proc` error classes. The fail-direction test covers only the negative side, \"error other than ENOENT, ESRCH or EACCES\". No test shows that EACCES on `exe`, or ENOENT/ESRCH on `exe` or on stat, leaves the unit evictable. No test shows that EACCES on `stat /proc/<pid>` makes the unit undetermined. Treating EACCES `exe` as undetermined is the failure that would hurt most. This host has non-dumpable same-user daemons (E5), so every unit would be skipped forever and the step would always warn. No listed assertion goes red on that change. The real-machine run only \"records\" outcomes and does not assert them. (confidence: 82)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "r4 body:112-119 vs 163-169 and 215-220 — The Bound says in-use units alone or non-unit content alone can keep a root over 40 GiB, and \"either case is reported as a warning\". O6 pins the unreachable-cap report and exit 1 only for non-unit content. The in-use case is asserted only as \"unit kept\". A stub that computes \"unreachable\" as `nonUnitBytes > cap` passes everything, and an in-use-pinned oversize root then exits 0 with no warning. Add a case: an in-use unit alone keeps the root over the cap, and the unreachable line and exit 1 are asserted. (confidence: 80)"
      },

        "kind": "should-fix",

The MANDATE proof, `du -sh` under the bound after a week, therefore depends on log output that is neither specified nor tested. Either specify and assert a per-root \"after\" total line, or say the observer runs `du` right after a gate. (confidence: 80)"

      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "`tasks/plans/…-r5.body.md` O6 \"Runner tests\" (the `:262` sentence \"a termination failure that latches `childCleanup.error` keep their existing outcomes\") — the new O3 exception is that a `terminationError` result stays `failed` and latches `childCleanup.error`. The O6 line was not touched by the delta and names no assertion for it. A runner that maps every non-zero advisory result, including `terminationError`, to `warning` would slip through. That is the I63 false-green. At BASE, `recordChildOutcome` copies `terminationError` onto the task, and `:773` refuses later starts once `childCleanup.error` is set. O6 should therefore assert that an advisory step whose supervisor reports unconfirmed termination (a) is recorded `failed`, not `warning`, (b) makes the run exit non-zero, (c) sets `childCleanup.error`, and (d) refuses later starts. The \"interrupt\" and \"termination failure\" clauses are currently one undifferentiated \"existing outcomes\" (confidence: 80)"
      },
      {
        "kind": "should-fix",
        "text": "O1 \"allocated blocks of every entry, by `lstat`\" and O6 \"Size cap\" — I found no case that distinguishes allocated blocks from apparent size. The only size cases are \"allocated files push a root over a small threshold\" and \"a hard-linked file is counted once\". If the walker reverted to `st_size`, both stay green. That would also leave directory and symlink blocks uncounted, which breaks the plan's `du` equivalence claim. A sparse file (large apparent size, near-zero blocks) that must not trip the cap would anchor this. Round 2's I8 had sparse files, but round 3's I23 replaced them without adding a negative case. The real-machine `du` before/after comparison isn't tied to the script's per-root total, so it doesn't cover this either (confidence: 80)"
      }
    ],

### lens-chess-semantics-r1.txt

NOT APPLICABLE: the listed files (`scripts/rust-target-eviction.mjs`, `scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs`, `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs`, `scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`, `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs`, `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs`, `package.json`, `.claude/rules/gate-tooling.md`) are all build and gate tooling. The mandate, f-20261017-04, is about evicting stale cargo artifacts under `src-tauri/target/`, `llvm-cov-target` and `mutants.out`. None of this touches the in-memory game tree, move paths, FEN or position identity, ply or colour parity, variation order, chess rules, or tree and header round trips. I did not read the plan or the source, since the file list alone shows this lens's class can't occur.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-correctness-r1.txt

Review of plan r1 for f-20261017-04, correctness lens only. I found nothing at or above 80 confidence, so no finding is reported as a blocker. The should-fix items below are listed with honest confidence scores, and the orchestrator will drop those under 80.

**Reads that were blocked or missing**
- `/tmp/drain-plan-drain-90a2aa88-slot0/build/plan-r1.md` was blocked by the sandbox: it is outside the allowed directories, and I did not try to get around that. I reviewed `tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md` instead. I can't confirm it matches r1.
- `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` and global CLAUDE.md rules 4, 6d and 12b were not read, for the same reason. I read the two named repo rule files in full.
- The FILES list is the plan's intended output. The new files don't exist at BASE, so I checked the plan's claims against the existing boundaries: `run-push-gates.mjs`, `run-push-gates-tests.mjs`, `check-gate-routing.mjs` and `run-backend-mutation.mjs`.

**Findings**

`[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:113-118 (O4) — The plan names no trigger or schedule phase for the pre-review step.`
- The trigger "whenever the run selects the `rust` or `bindings` block" has no meaning in pre-review. There `blocks` is an empty `Set` (`run-push-gates.mjs:1020`), and `selectedByBlocks` returns false for any `blocks`-gated entry (`:604`).

- The right condition is that `rustLane` (`windows-clippy`) is selected (`:1097`).
- "Before `setup-rust`" is ambiguous because the runner addresses P0 by index (`PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0[0]` and `p0[1]`, `:1144`, `:1157-1158`). A step inserted at index 1 would be run as `setupRust` and `bash scripts/setup-rust.sh` would never run. The helper `scheduledFakeCommands` (tests `:128-132`) and the `beforeStepNames` deepEqual (`:2066`) pin the same order.

`[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:103-109 vs run-push-gates.mjs:1132-1133, run-push-gates-tests.mjs:1442 — O3's "never turns a gate red" is only a property of the script. The runner doesn't enforce it.`
- A P0 step that exits non-zero is added to `stepResults`, and `firstFailure(stepResults)` sets the run's exit code. The test "P0/P1 failures take exit precedence over lane failures" pins that behaviour.
- A spawn failure (exit 127), a signal, or an uncaught exception outside the per-unit try (for example `git worktree list` failing, or an EMFILE error) therefore fails the whole gate.

`[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:99-101 (O2) — "A partly removed unit is still a valid cargo directory" depends on removal order, and the plan gives none.`
- If `build/<pkg>/output` and `.fingerprint` survive while `build/<pkg>/out/*` is gone, cargo treats the build script as fresh and rustc then fails on a missing `OUT_DIR` file. Cargo's freshness check covers the `output` file but, as far as I know, not the `out/` contents.
- This is reachable in two ways: SIGTERM from agent-gate scope teardown mid-delete, and the unremovable-entry case that O6 tests and then continues past.
- Deleting `.fingerprint/` first, and treating any unremovable entry as a unit failure, makes every intermediate state rebuild cleanly.
- (confidence: 65, from knowledge of cargo's freshness check, not from a probe)

`[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:84-87, 128-144 — The established bound is per unit, but the mandated proof is `du -sh` of each whole `target/`.`
- One target can hold `debug`, `release`, `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu/debug` and `llvm-cov-target/debug`, each up to 32 GiB, plus non-unit content such as `target/doc` and profraw files in `llvm-cov-target/`. The plan therefore bounds a target at roughly 128 GiB or more, close to the 123 GB defect.
- The plan states no per-target number, and O6 and the Phase 1 PROOF COMMAND don't check one.
- (confidence: 70)

`[should-fix] O1 / O2 — The predicate is evaluated outside the lock, and the size metric is under-specified.`
- Idle and oversize are decided before the `flock`, and the plan doesn't say to re-check them while the lock is held. A cargo that builds and exits between the check and the lock gets its fresh unit wiped.
- Oversize eviction can also hit a unit while its cargo is in the test phase. Cargo has released the build lock by then, and tests that spawn sibling binaries from `target/debug` would fail.
- "Apparent size" is ambiguous. Rustc's incremental sessions hard-link objects between sessions. A summed `st_size` double-counts them, while the 17 GB calibration came from `du`, which de-duplicates. The 32 GiB threshold could then trip on a unit that is really fine.
- The idle check cannot see no-op warm runs, which write no mtime. Only an actual rebuild moves the `deps` mtime, as the plan's probe shows.
- (confidence: 55–60 each, so informational)

`[nit] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:81-83 — "Just under twice the largest fresh unit (17 GB)" is arithmetically wrong.`
- 17 GB is about 15.8 GiB, so twice that is 31.7 GiB. 32 GiB is just over twice, and the GB/GiB mix obscures the margin.
- (confidence: 85)

**Checked and consistent**
- Routing: `rust:target:evict:test` chained into `gates:contract:check` satisfies the `:(check|test)$` reachability rule. `rust:target:evict` is reachable through the schedule.
- Mutation fence: the fence path is per checkout. `prepareCache` only checks ancestors and aliasing, so emptied units don't affect it.
- A cargo that is waiting for the lock proceeds correctly into a unit emptied in place.

### lens-correctness-r2.txt

The plan fails on one point: the `/proc` working-directory check, as specified, makes the idle rule evict nothing on a normal host. I couldn't run the `/proc` probe because the sandbox denied it. That finding rests on documented kernel behavior, not a measurement here.

**(1) Open issues witnessed**

- I1 CLOSED. `runSerialStep` pushes every step into `stepResults`, and `firstFailure(stepResults)` at `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:1290` sets the exit code from it. O3 makes the eviction entry advisory, so that path is covered. The failed-task trailer loop at `:984-987` must also skip advisory tasks, or a "push gate failed: rust-target-eviction" line appears next to an exit code of 0. Phase 1 lists the O6 runner tests that exercise this.
- I2 CLOSED. `git grep` shows `PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0[0]` at `:1144` and `p0[1]` at `:1157-1158` as the only index consumers. `pushGateScheduleCommands` flattens the whole list, and `preReviewGateScheduleCommands` flattens `["p1","lanes"]`, so pre-review has no P0. O4's push-only wiring with lookup by name resolves it.
- I4 CLOSED for safety. Re-checking under the lock, plus the size cap for the own checkout and the idle rule with a cwd check for other checkouts, closes the gap where cargo frees its locks while tests run. The cwd check's fail direction is a separate defect: see D1.
- I5 CLOSED. The cap is now per target root, evicting the largest unit first, and the Bound is explicitly scoped to the start of a push gate in the gating checkout.
- I12 CLOSED for safety. The own checkout is exempt from the idle rule, and other checkouts get the cwd check. The liveness problem is D1.
- I18 CLOSED. Counting each inode once by device and inode number removes the hard-link double count.
- I19 CLOSED. Removing `.fingerprint` first means every later partial state has no fingerprints, so cargo rebuilds it. I checked this against `.fingerprint`, `deps`, `build`, `incremental` and the unit's top-level outputs. A kill during the `.fingerprint` removal itself is also safe, because a missing fingerprint file counts as dirty.

**(2) Defects in the delta**

[blocker] O2 "Fail direction is skip" and O1 idle rule (plan lines 95-99 of the delta) — The "unreadable `/proc` entry for a foreign checkout → skip the unit" clause contradicts the idle rule's cwd check on a normal host.
- **Why:** `readlink /proc/<pid>/cwd` fails with EACCES for every process owned by another uid (root daemons, `systemd`, kernel threads). It also fails for same-uid processes that are non-dumpable, such as `ssh-agent` and `gpg-agent`. A process cannot tell which checkout an unreadable entry would have pointed at, so the only implementable reading is "any unreadable entry skips the unit".
- **Input:** a stale side-lane worktree with no process inside it, on an ordinary desktop session. The scan hits an unreadable entry, so the unit is skipped on every run and nothing is ever evicted.
- **Race:** a process that exits between the directory listing and the `readlink` gives ENOENT or ESRCH, which the same clause turns into a spurious skip.
- **Consequence:** the idle rule and the real-machine evidence ("three side-lane worktrees exceed 7 idle days") can never be met on the real host. Fixture tests that use same-uid, readable processes would still pass.
- **Required:** the plan must define which unreadable entries count as ambiguous. For example, ignore entries for another uid or ones that vanished, and skip only on a failure that cannot be attributed to a foreign process. (confidence: 85)

**(3) Other blockers anywhere in the plan**

None beyond D1.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

None at or above 80 confidence.

### lens-error-handling-r1.txt

I reviewed the plan from `tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md`. I couldn't read the `/tmp/.../plan-r1.md` path you named, and I assumed the two are the same revision.

I also couldn't read `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or the global `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` rules 4, 6d and 12b. The sandbox denied both, so I judged against the lens text and the two rule files in this worktree. The new scripts don't exist at BASE, so I traced the failure paths against the existing runner code.

**Findings**

[should-fix] plan O3/O4 (`2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:103-118`) — O3 promises eviction "never fails a gate", but O4 wires it as a P0 step through `runSerialStep`. In `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:1050-1056` every step lands in `stepResults`, and `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:1290` and `:1132` turn the first `failed` or `interrupted` step into the run's exit code. An uncaught throw, an OOM kill, SIGTERM or a missing package script would make `rust-target-eviction` fail, and the gate would go red over housekeeping. "Exits 0 unless its invocation is malformed" covers only per-unit errors, not the process failing. The plan has no top-level catch-all that forces exit 0 and no advisory-step semantics in the runner. It also doesn't say that a failed or interrupted eviction step must not skip lanes, as `setup-rust` and the mutation guard do. O6 has no runner-level test where the step exits non-zero and the gates stay green. (confidence: 88)

[should-fix] plan O3 (`:105-109`) — Eviction failures are invisible when the run passes. Per-unit lines go to the step's log file. `printSummary` (`scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:948-988`) prints a status row for each step, but only failed or interrupted steps get a log tail. So "failed with error" and "skipped: lock held" produce no output the user or drain sees. A permanently failing unit, for example root-owned files from a container build or a stuck lock, would let target directories grow again with no signal until the one-week `du` check. The plan should specify a one-line warning in the runner output, or a non-green status such as "passed (warnings)", whenever any unit failed. It should also say what happens when the same unit is skipped on every run. (confidence: 85)

[should-fix] plan O2 (`:92-101`) — The lifetime of the process that holds the `flock` is not specified. Node can't hold a lock itself, so it has to use a `flock` child. If that child outlives a killed eviction process (SIGKILL, scope teardown, runner interrupt), it keeps the lock on `.cargo-lock` and every later cargo build in that checkout blocks silently. That matches the async-resource rule: every spawn needs an owner and cleanup on every exit path. The plan should say the removal runs inside the lock holder, so the lock dies with it. For example, `flock -n <file> -c <remove>`, or children that are killed on exit and on signals. It should also add a test where the evictor is killed mid-removal and the lock is released. (confidence: 82)

[should-fix] plan O2/O1 (`:78-101`) — Fail direction on ambiguous inputs is not stated, and for a deletion it must be "skip and report". Four cases are open:
- **`flock -n -E 75`:** The probe distinguishes only 75 (held) from 0. Any other non-zero exit, such as ENOLCK on a filesystem without lock support, must skip the unit and report an error. It must not be read as "not held".
- **Unreadable mtime or size:** A failed `stat` or walk that defaults to 0 or epoch would make the unit look idle and get it deleted.
- **Fence check:** Only ENOENT on `.mutation-in-progress` means "no fence". EACCES or any other error must skip.
- **`git worktree list --porcelain`:** It can fail, or list prunable or missing paths. The plan doesn't say whether to evict only the current checkout or nothing.

(confidence: 80)

[should-fix] plan O2 (`:100-101`) — "A partly removed unit is still a valid, smaller cargo directory … needs no recovery step" is asserted, not traced. If `.fingerprint` survives while `deps/*` or `build/*/output` are removed, cargo's freshness check may not detect every missing output. That would give a build failure or stale artifact, which the gate would then report as a Rust failure. The plan should fix a removal order that never leaves a fingerprint without its outputs, for example `.fingerprint` first. It should also add an interrupted-eviction test that runs a real `cargo check` on a tiny crate. Until then, treat this as a named limitation: I couldn't trace cargo's internals. (confidence: 78 as a defect, reported because it underpins the "no recovery step" claim)

**Named limitation (below the reporting threshold)**
- A held lock proves a cargo build is running. It doesn't prove the unit is unused. `cargo test` and `cargo llvm-cov` can release the build lock before running test binaries or reading profraw. On the oversize path, which doesn't wait for 7 idle days, eviction could delete files out from under a running test in another checkout. The probe only showed the lock held during a `build.rs`. (confidence: 60)

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-error-handling-r2.txt

**(1) Open issues**

- I1 CLOSED. `runStep` already turns a spawn failure or crash into `code 1` and `status "failed"` (`scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:818-833`). The exit code comes only from `firstFailure(stepResults)` (`:990`, `:1290`), and `setup-rust` skipping reads only `setupRust?.status` (`:1169`, `:1187`). So the plan's "advisory entry, result never changes exit or admission" is a well-defined change. The O6 runner tests cover non-zero exit, spawn failure and crash. The executor needs a status other than `failed`, which O3 implies by saying "warning".
- I17 CLOSED. The remover runs inside the nested `flock -n` holders, so it inherits the locked file descriptors and the locks end with it. A killed parent can't leave a lock held after the remover ends. O6 has a kill case for this.
- [should-fix] I19 NOT CLOSED. The order "`.fingerprint` first" only protects an interrupted removal. The text "an entry that cannot be removed makes that unit a failure, and the next unit continues" doesn't say whether removal in the failing unit stops or goes on best-effort. If `.fingerprint` can't be fully removed (a root-owned or read-only entry) and removal continues into `deps` and `incremental`, the unit ends up with fingerprints but no outputs. That is the I19 hazard, and the plan's rationale "any later partial state has no fingerprints" would be false. Fix: if `.fingerprint` is not fully removed, remove nothing else and fail the unit. Name the stop rule in O2. (confidence: 80)
- I20 CLOSED for failed units. O3 gives exit 1 for a failed unit, and the summary shows a warning with the log tail. Currently `printSummary` prints a log tail only for `failed` or `interrupted` (`:967-969`), so the plan correctly requires a new status. The related gap for skip-on-error is D2 below.
- I21 CLOSED for fail direction. "Fail direction is skip" is stated, with the listed ambiguous observations. The gaps in how those skips surface are D1 and D2.

**(2) Defects in the delta**

- [should-fix] O1/O2 — "an unreadable `/proc` entry for a foreign checkout → skip" is undefined, and taken literally it disables the idle rule on every real machine. Per proc(5), `/proc/<pid>/cwd` of another user's process needs a ptrace read check and gives EACCES. A workstation always has root-owned processes (systemd, dockerd), and an unreadable cwd can't be tied to a checkout. Read that way, every foreign unit is skipped on every run and the step exits 0. The bound then silently never holds, and only the one-week `du` would show it. The plan must say which entries count. A process of another uid is out of scope. ENOENT means the process exited and is ignored. EACCES on a same-uid process is ambiguous and skips. I could not probe `/proc` here (the command was denied), so this rests on proc(5) semantics. The O6 real-machine run would likely catch it, but the failure contract is unspecified. (confidence: 82)
- [should-fix] O2/O3 — Skips caused by errors exit 0 and are invisible in a passing run. This covers a `flock` exit other than 0 or 75 (including a missing binary), a failed `stat` or size walk, a fence-check error, and an unreadable `/proc` entry. It also covers a `git worktree list --porcelain` failure, which silently drops every foreign checkout and isn't a unit line at all. The runner prints a log tail only for non-zero steps (`:967-969`), so these show as a green "passed" with no signal. This is the I20 problem for the skip class. Fix: separate expected skips (held lock, fence, not yet idle, platform) from undetermined skips (an observation error). Count undetermined skips and a listing failure toward exit 1, so the advisory warning shows. (confidence: 85)

None.

- [should-fix] plan r3 lines 292–295 (Risks, reader bullet) — The claim that a reader "follows a write within seconds, so the unit is never idle" contradicts E2: a fully fresh cargo run writes nothing in the unit.
  - Trace in `scripts/rust-branch-coverage.mjs:214-247`: `cargo llvm-cov --no-report` exits, then the script reads `llvm-cov-target/debug/deps` and runs `llvm-cov export` on a binary in it.
  - In that window no cargo lock is held, and the process running is the toolchain's `llvm-cov`, not an executable inside the unit. So both in-use tests are false.
  - If the checkout's last compile was 7 or more days ago and this run was fully fresh, another checkout's eviction step can empty `deps`. The coverage lane in that checkout then fails with an ENOENT or "executable was not found" error. Nothing in that checkout's log points to the eviction.
  - The plan files this as a known limit on a false premise (I12 says it is "covered by the in-use test"). The plan should either give no-compile runs a signal the idle clock can see, or restate the limit with this failure mode. (confidence: 80)
- [should-fix] plan r3 lines 91–93 and 104–108 (O1 cap rule and Bound) — The unreachable-cap case is reported and exits 1, but the plan never says whether eviction runs first. Read literally, "evicted largest first until the root is at most 40 GiB" evicts every non-in-use unit when non-unit content or in-use units alone exceed the cap.
  - Every gate in the own checkout would then start with a full cold build. The cap would still be unreachable, so the same wipe would repeat on every run.
  - The safe direction is to test reachability first and evict nothing when the cap cannot be met. The O6 case ("non-unit content alone exceeds the threshold") asserts only the report and the exit code, so it passes either way. (confidence: 80)

None.

The plan gives them different outcomes: "unit failed, may be partly removed" versus "undetermined skip, contents intact". CR-2 only reserves 75. A remover that exits 1 after partial removal could be logged as "skipped, contents intact". Both cases exit 1 and warn, so the harm is a misleading log line. (confidence: 75)

1. [blocker] `.plan-…-r4.body.md:170-176` (O3 Runner) — The rewritten bullet maps "a non-zero exit, a spawn failure or a crash" to status `warning`. `firstFailure` ignores that status, so it cannot set the exit code. It then says the termination-failure latch "keeps its existing handling" without saying the step's status stays `failed` when that happens. In `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs`, `runCommand` returns `{code: 1, terminationError}` on both termination paths (`:715`, `:724-728`) and latches `childCleanup.error` (`:714`, `:723`).
   - Today the latch only blocks later starts through `blockedStartReason` (`:773`). The non-zero exit comes from the step being `failed` and `firstFailure` (`:1290-1291`), and from nothing else.
   - `cleanupFailed` (`:1343`) is set only by `terminateAll` or `signalForwarding.termination` throwing, not by the latch.
   - Read literally, a termination failure in the advisory eviction step becomes a `warning`. Every later lane and step is then skipped as "child cleanup is unconfirmed", and the run exits 0 with no Rust proof run. That is a failure returned as success on a safety gate. The step also leaves a possibly live `flock` holder, which is the case the latch exists for.
   - `printSummary` (`:967-982`) prints `terminationError` only for `failed` or `interrupted` rows, so the warning row would also hide the "Child termination failed" text.
   - The plan must state that an advisory result carrying `terminationError`, or one that latched `childCleanup.error`, keeps status `failed` (exit 1, trailer, termination text). 2. [should-fix] `.plan-…-r4.body.md:97-103, 218-220` (O1 cap and its O6 case, from the I58 fix) — When non-unit content alone exceeds 40 GiB, the rule evicts every evictable unit and then reports the cap as unreachable. O6 asserts this as the intended behaviour.
   - That eviction cannot reach the bound, because the cap is already unreachable.
   - It wipes the own checkout's warm cache on every Rust push gate, which is the checkout about to build. The result is a cold-with-sccache build on every gate, plus an exit-1 warning, until someone removes the non-unit content by hand.
   - It is a destructive action that is known up front to be futile. The fail direction should be to evict nothing and report the unreachable cap when non-unit content alone is at or over the cap. The same question applies when in-use units plus non-unit content exceed it. The plan should state which way it fails.
   - The same goes for the O6 case that asserts evict-all. (confidence: 80)

None beyond item 1.

**(1) Open issues**

- I61 CLOSED. O1 now decides reachability before evicting anything: if in-use units plus non-unit content alone exceed 40 GiB, nothing is evicted. The step logs the unreachable cap line and exits 1 (O3). That removes the futile cold rebuild on every gate, and O6 adds both cases (non-unit content alone, and an in-use unit alone). The fix creates a gap, listed in section 2.
- I63 CLOSED. In `runCommand`, both `terminationError` returns latch `childCleanup.error` first (`scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:714-715` and `:723-727`). `runStep` copies the error into the task via `recordChildOutcome` (`:826`, `:280`). The new O3 text makes the `warning` mapping conditional on no `terminationError`, so `runStep` keeps the status `failed` (`:827-833`). `firstFailure` then drives the exit code (`:1290-1291`), and `blockedStartReason` keeps queued starts blocked (`:769-775`). An interrupt still wins as `interrupted`, because the `requestedSignal` branch is checked first. The `superviseProcess` seam is injectable, so the O6 runner test can reject `supervisor.done` to cover the non-interrupt latch path.

**(2) Defects introduced by the delta**

[should-fix] plan O1 "Own checkout" and O3 — The cap rule decides "unreachable" once, before eviction. It never says what happens when a unit is skipped during removal (confidence: 80). Under O2, a unit can turn out to be in use at the re-check, or have a lock held, after selection. That skip is an expected skip, so the exit stays 0. The root can then finish above 40 GiB with only a per-root total line and no warning. The O1 Bound promises that in-use content over the cap "is reported as a warning". The plan also doesn't say whether largest-first selection continues to the next-largest unit after such a skip or stops at the pre-computed set. If it stops, a smaller evictable unit that would have reached the cap is left alone. Fix: after the loop, compare the actual per-root total with the cap. If it is still over, report the unreachable cap and exit 1. Evict by actual freed bytes, and continue past skipped units.

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None.

None at or above 80 confidence.

The real-machine concurrent-build run and the three timing runs are verification the Proof and Open-question text ask for. No criterion pushes the work past the mandate. The two repo rule files are outside the sandbox path, so I read the copies in this worktree, which is at the same BASE (385287ef). Nothing was written or run beyond `git`/`grep` reads.

**(1) Open issues**

**(2) Defects introduced by the DELTA**

- The `/proc/<pid>/exe` in-use test, the "unreachable cap" line, the five idle-signal cases and the fail-direction cases are all tied to a reopened or fixed issue (I4, I12, I21, I25). Each one covers failure semantics the MANDATE's "what it keeps" question needs, so none is deletable.
- The added "growth of the root" measurement rides on the same cold-run timing, so it costs nothing extra.

**(3) Blockers**
None.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [should-fix] O2 Skips, `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress`, and the "mutants.out/backend/cargo-target" root in O1 — The plan re-spells two path literals that `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs` already owns (`fencePath` at line 35, the cache join in `prepareCache` at line 352). If either changes, the fence skip stops matching and the eviction step would treat a live mutation cache as an ordinary root without saying so. The module is importable: it has `export`s and an `isEntrypoint` guard. The smaller diff exports those two values from `run-backend-mutation.mjs` and imports them, which adds about two lines there and saves a second copy to keep in sync. The plan lists that file as read-only, so it never schedules this. `run-frontend-mutation.mjs:34` has the same kind of literal, but the plan doesn't touch it. (confidence: 80)

- [should-fix] O2 "Platform" bullet, and the O6 line "The non-Linux branch exits 0 through the injected platform" — This is a speculative branch with an injected seam and a dedicated test. Three things make it unreachable or redundant:
  - `pnpm gates:push` is an `agent-gate` command that needs a systemd `agents.slice` scope and fails closed with no in-place fallback (`gate-tooling.md`, "Scheduling and placement"), so a non-Linux host can't reach this step.
  - The threat model puts CI and non-Linux hosts out of scope "beyond not failing a gate", and the advisory wrapper in O3 already guarantees that for any crash.
  - The step would also fail closed on non-Linux, because `flock` and `/proc` are missing and the observation is undetermined.

  Deleting the bullet, the `platform` parameter and the one test loses no acceptance coverage. (confidence: 80)

**Named limitations (under 80, not scored as defects)**

The idle rule could rely on a per-gate stamp in the gating root, but that would not protect the own-checkout cap against a second concurrent gate. Round 2 adopted the scan against evidence E1, so I'm leaving it.
- The Decisions text rejecting a size cap on other checkouts ("cargo frees its locks while tests run, so a cap there can delete a running gate's test binaries") is now partly stale. The in-use test covers that case for the own-checkout cap. It is a consistency point, not bloat, but it affects whether the two-rule split is the minimal one.

### lens-plan-r1.txt

**Limitations**
- I could not read `/tmp/drain-plan-drain-90a2aa88-slot0/build/plan-r1.md`. The file tools are confined to the lens worktree and `tasks/plans`, and Bash was denied for that path. I reviewed `tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md` in the drain worktree instead, and I cannot confirm it is identical to r1. Round 1 has no re-review block, so I made no scoped-coverage claim.
- Everything else I read at BASE 385287ef. That covers the two named rule files and `d-20261002-01`. Nothing in the plan contradicts those decisions.
- I did not re-run the plan's `flock` probe. I took its result as stated.

**Findings**

```
[blocker] plan:103-109 (O3), scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:1052-1056,1290-1291 — O3 says eviction "never
fails, skips or delays a gate lane". The runner does not give that guarantee. runSerialStep pushes the
step into stepResults, and firstFailure(stepResults) sets exitCode. Any non-zero exit from the new P0
step therefore turns the gate red. That includes an uncaught exception, a failed `git worktree list`,
and pnpm or node exiting 127. O3 guarantees exit 0 only inside the script, and O6 tests only the
script ("non-Linux / missing flock exits 0", one unit failing). Nothing proves the runner still passes,
or at least does not fail, when the step exits non-zero. Either the script needs a catch-all that
exits 0, or the schedule entry needs an advisory flag that the runner honours. The plan names
neither, and no test would go red if either were missing. (confidence: 75)

[blocker] plan:111-118 (O4), scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:150-153,303-305,1144-1158; scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs:533-535 —
O4 says "a new P0 step in both modes". PRE_REVIEW_GATE_SCHEDULE has no `p0` key, only `p1` and
`lanes`. `preReviewGateScheduleCommands` flattens only ["p1","lanes"], and the routing checker
consumes that. The pre-review branch (1128-1141) has no P0 stage at all. In push mode the runner
addresses P0 by position: `p0[0]` is the guard (1144) and `p0[1]` is setup-rust (1157-1158).
Inserting the step "before setup-rust" at index 1 makes line 1157 select the eviction step as
setup-rust, which silently breaks the `requires: setup-rust` lanes. The plan lists
check-gate-routing.mjs as "only if its route table needs the new command". In fact the flatten phase
list in run-push-gates.mjs must change, or the pre-review command is never routed-checked. The plan
should state this schedule-shape change and the positional consumers. It should also say the
pre-review step runs only when a rustCompilation lane is selected, before `getRustEnvironment`
at line 1103. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] plan:128-144 (O6), scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs:132,843 — "the gate-runner tests accept
the new P0 step in both modes" is not a proof that can fail. The ordering and selection obligations
need assertions that would go red if wrong:
- The step precedes setup-rust and runs only for rust or bindings.
- Contract-only and frontend-only runs omit it.
- Pre-review runs it only when a Rust lane is selected, and before that lane starts.
- A failing step does not change the exit code (see the first blocker).
The exact-list fixtures at :132 and :843 catch an unconditional add. They do not catch a missing or
misordered step. (confidence: 70)

[should-fix] plan:90-101 (O2), scripts/rust-branch-coverage.mjs:204-245 — "A lock that is already held means
cargo is running" is false after the build. Cargo releases its build locks before test execution.
`cargo llvm-cov --no-report` writes profraw files after that point. Then rust-branch-coverage.mjs
reads `llvm-cov-target/debug/deps` executables and the profiles once cargo has exited. In that window
an oversize unit has fresh mtimes and a free lock, so another checkout's gate start can wipe it
mid-test or mid-export. The idle predicate cannot hit this, because a unit written within 7 days is
not idle. The oversize predicate can. The consequence is a spurious red gate and an unexplained
ENOENT. The plan should add a recency guard on the oversize path, or state this as a known limit.
(confidence: 55)

[should-fix] plan:84-86 (O1) vs MANDATE Proof — the proof is "`du -sh` of every `target/` and `mutants.out`
stays under the chosen bound". The plan's bound is per unit (7 idle days, 32 GiB). A target root can
hold several units (`debug`, `release`, `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu/{debug,release}`, `llvm-cov-target/debug`).
Non-unit content such as `target/doc` and `target/tmp` is never evicted. The worst case is therefore
about 5 × 32 GiB, which is the size of the current problem. No total per-`target/` figure is stated,
so the one-week observation has nothing to compare against. O6 also defers that observation to a
later finding. State the total bound the finding's proof is checked against. (confidence: 65)

[nit] plan:195-197 — Phase 1 adds a new rule section but leaves the operative sentence at
.claude/rules/gate-tooling.md:24-27 unchanged. That sentence says the runner "runs the mutation guard
first (always) and `setup-rust` for Rust or bindings". It will be stale once the step exists, and the
rule's own policy is to delete superseded operative prose. Also "the reference to this decision" has
no named decision id or file in the Phase 1 file list. (confidence: 50)

[nit] plan:130-136 (O6) — the per-case unit-test list (symlinked root versus unit, unremovable entry,
lock files survive) is test-level detail below the obligation. It could move to the phase brief.
(confidence: 40)
```

**Checked and fine**
- The named symbols exist: `PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0`, the `setup-rust` entry's `blocks`, `prepareCache`, and the `mutation-in-progress` fence.
- `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` is a depth-1 unit, and the other `target/*` units sit at depth ≤ 2.
- The `rust:target:evict:test` script name needs no extra routing, because it is chained into `gates:contract:check`.
- Rejecting a shared target directory and leaving worktree removal to agent-kit do not conflict with `d-20261002-01` or `d-20260930-03`.

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-plan-r2.txt

**Coverage limit:** I did not run the `/proc/<pid>/cwd` permission probe. The sandbox denied the probes that required approval. The cwd finding below relies on documented Linux behavior and a plan-text reading, not a probe on this machine.

## (1) Open issues

- I1 CLOSED: O3 now makes the step advisory. The current behavior it has to override is real: `firstFailure(stepResults)` at `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:990`, with call sites at `:1132` and `:1290`. The contract and its runner test cases (non-zero exit, spawn failure, crash) can fail against a stub that doesn't implement the advisory handling.
- I2 CLOSED: The positional `p0[0]`/`p0[1]` reads are at `run-push-gates.mjs:1144,1157`, and `PRE_REVIEW_GATE_SCHEDULE` has no `p0`. O4 gives push mode only and lookup by name. Phase 1 lists `run-push-gates.mjs`.
- I3 CLOSED: The O6 runner cases cover ordering, block selection, advisory failure modes and name lookup. Existing exact command-set assertions in `run-push-gates-tests.mjs` (around `:1051`, `:1250-1277`) will go red. That file is in the plan's file list.
- [should-fix] I4 NOT CLOSED (residual, confidence 55): The idle rule now has the cwd check, but the own-checkout size cap has no protection against a second process in the same checkout. E1 shows cargo frees its locks while test binaries run. A second session or a by-hand `cargo test` in the gating checkout can lose its test binaries to a cap eviction. O1 says "this checkout's gate is about to use its units", which assumes a sole user. The threat model names "Felix by hand" and "a side lane" as concurrent actors. Either apply the cwd check to processes outside the runner's own process tree, or state this as a known limit in Risks.
- I5 CLOSED on per-root scope. A new metric issue is under (2).
- I6 CLOSED: Phase 1 corrects the sentence at `gate-tooling.md:24-27` and names the decision id the adopter records.
- I8, I9, I10, I11, I12, I13, I14, I16, I17, I18, I19, I20 CLOSED. Each correction is stated in O1, O2, O3 or O6 and each has an assertion that fails against a no-op, lock-ignoring or own-checkout-only stub.
- [should-fix] I21 NOT CLOSED (confidence 70): O2 says "an unreadable `/proc` entry for a foreign checkout" skips the unit. An unreadable `/proc/<pid>/cwd` cannot be attributed to any checkout, because the link target is exactly what cannot be read. For a non-root user, `readlink` on other users' processes (root, kernel threads, system daemons) fails with EACCES. A reader must then pick one of two readings. If any unreadable entry skips every foreign unit, the idle rule never fires, and the real-machine run and the one-week bound fail. If unreadable entries are ignored, the rule contradicts the fail-direction principle. The plan must say which processes count, for example same-uid only. O6 has no case for an unreadable or foreign-uid entry.

## (2) Defects in the DELTA hunks

- [should-fix] `PLAN-r2:70-104` O1/Bound — The bound's metric differs from the proof's metric (confidence 65). O1 says sizes count each inode once "as `du` does". O6 builds the cap test on sparse files "give a real apparent size over the cap". The finding's proof is `du -sh`, which reports allocated blocks. For trees with many small files, allocated size exceeds apparent size, so `du` can exceed 40 GiB while the apparent size is at most 40 GiB. Name the measure (st_size or allocated blocks) and make it match the `du` observation, or state the margin.
- [should-fix] `PLAN-r2:97-101` Bound — The bound is stated "at the start of every push gate", but the proof reads `du` at arbitrary times (confidence 60). The gate then builds into the root, and nothing evicts until the next gate. The plan says the one-week `du -sh` observation is checked against these numbers. That check can fail honestly at about 40 GiB plus one gate's growth. State the tolerance or the observation point.
- [should-fix] `PLAN-r2:138-142` O3 — "Never skips or blocks a later step" conflicts with the existing latch (confidence 60). A rejected supervisor completion sets `childCleanup.error` (`run-push-gates.mjs:714,723`), and `blockedStartReason` (`:773`) then skips every later start. An advisory remover whose termination cannot be confirmed leaves a possibly live `flock` holder, so keeping that latch is correct. The plan's wording should carve it out, saying that termination failure keeps its existing handling like an interrupt. As written, a literal implementation either violates the claim or removes the safety latch.
- [should-fix] `PLAN-r2:86-95` O1 idle rule — A cwd signal keeps a unit forever (confidence 60). The main checkout is the finding's headline at 123 GB. It nearly always has a shell, editor or orchestrator process with its cwd inside it. When gates run from a worktree, the main checkout is a foreign checkout and is never idle-evicted. It is bounded only if someone gates in the main checkout itself. Risks lists the reverse case (a reader with its cwd elsewhere) but not this one. Also, the O6 real-machine claim "three side-lane worktrees exceed 7 idle days" will show evictions only if no process has its cwd in those worktrees. Record the actual outcome, or state the limit.

## (3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None. Every named symbol I checked exists and behaves as the plan assumes:
- `PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0`
- `flattenGateSchedule` (`:307`), which accepts an extra advisory field
- `selectedByBlocks`
- `printSummary` and `runSerialStep`
- `prepareCache` (`run-backend-mutation.mjs:351`) and the fence path (`:35`)
- `check-gate-routing.mjs` `routeCommands` (`:285`)
- the `gates:contract:check` chain in `package.json:20`

The decisions `d-20260930-03` and `d-20261002-01` concern worker sizing and launcher placement. An advisory P0 step does not reopen them.

## LATE OBSERVATIONS

- [nit] `PLAN-r2:108-113` O2 — A nested `flock -n -E 75` chain around the remover shares exit 75 with the remover's own exit space. The remover must not exit 75, or the outcome parser can misread a removal failure as "lock held" (confidence 50).

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-plan-r3.txt

**Round 3 review of the `rust-target-eviction` plan (r3)**

I could not run live probes: Bash was restricted to read-only git and grep, and `~/.claude` was out of reach. I relied on the recorded E1, E2 and E5 evidence, and I did not re-verify `kit worktree remove` or next-finding §5.

## (1) Open issues

- I4 CLOSED. O1 "In use" (PLAN:80-87) and the O2 re-check (PLAN:123-124) cover the test phase under both rules. The millisecond gap between test binaries is stated as a known limit (PLAN:296-298). `cargo test --all-targets` (`scripts/gate-receipt.mjs:42`) means no doctest process reads `deps` unprotected.
- I21 CLOSED. EACCES and ENOENT no longer skip every unit (PLAN:136-142). The stated rationale for ignoring EACCES is false, though (D1 below).
- I12 CLOSED. The own checkout is exempt from the idle rule, and E2's no-compile gap is covered by the in-use test.
- [should-fix] I24 NOT CLOSED. The tolerance is "at most one gate run's writes" (PLAN:106-107). That is false for any checkout that builds between gates, such as a build worktree running `cargo test` repeatedly or the main checkout under `tauri dev`. The one-week `du -sh` check could fail with no defect, or pass vacuously (confidence: 60).
- I23 CLOSED. Allocated blocks with each inode counted once matches `du` (PLAN:75-78).
- I25 CLOSED. The latch (`run-push-gates.mjs:714,723,773`) is kept, and PLAN:159-161 says so.
- I26 CLOSED. The cwd check is removed and the rejection is recorded (PLAN:259-263).
- I14 CLOSED. The probe-crate runs under `cargo build` and `cargo test` (PLAN:230-236) go red against a stub that ignores the lock or the executable test.
- I28 CLOSED. `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:18` and `:888` pin `CONTRACT_CHAIN`. A git grep found only `package.json` and that test file carrying the chain, and Phase 1 names the test file.
- I29 CLOSED. The "Fail direction" cases (PLAN:211-213) would fail against a fail-open implementation.
- I30 CLOSED. "Order and stop rule" (PLAN:205-208) asserts the fingerprint-first order.
- I31 CLOSED. There is one idle case per signal (PLAN:192-194).
- I32 CLOSED. Non-unit content and the unreachable cap are in O1 Bound, O3 and O6 (PLAN:197-201).
- [blocker] I33 NOT CLOSED. The mechanism is stated ("canonical paths on both sides", PLAN:142). O6 has no case that fails when paths are compared as spelled. The "In use" and discovery fixtures (PLAN:189-196) are plain temp directories. A spelled-path comparison would pass every listed test. It fails open on the one predicate that protects a running test binary. O6's own rule that every assertion must go red against a stub (PLAN:184) is not met here (confidence: 75).
- I19 CLOSED. The stop rule and the second-pass failure case are in PLAN:127-131 and 205-208.
- I34 CLOSED. Undetermined skips, listing failure and an unreachable cap all exit 1, and the runner shows a warning (PLAN:149-161).

## (2) Defects introduced by the delta

- **D1 [should-fix]** PLAN:85-87 and E5 (full plan, Reviews) claim that an unreadable `exe` "cannot be a cargo-built binary". `src-tauri/src/db/encoding.rs:604` calls `prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)` inside a cargo-built test executable. The mutation runner sets that mode (`run-backend-mutation.mjs:41`). Such a process returns EACCES on `/proc/<pid>/exe`, so the in-use test ignores it.
  - Today only the `.mutation-in-progress` fence protects that case, and only for `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target`.
  - The plan should say the fence is the protection and drop the impossibility claim. It should also add a case where a non-dumpable process in a mutation cache is still protected (confidence: 80).
- **D2 [should-fix]** The Decisions rejection of "a size cap on other checkouts" (PLAN:267-268) rests on "cargo frees its locks while tests run". The delta's in-use test removes that premise, and the own-checkout cap relies on the same test.
  - The asymmetry is now unjustified.
  - It leaves a third class with no bound: a checkout that builds actively but never runs a Rust push gate, such as the main checkout under dev builds. The push skill says builds live in worktrees, while "small edits stay in the main checkout" (SKILL.md:475). Such a checkout is never idle and never "own", so it can grow without limit.
  - The Goal (PLAN:5) promises "every cargo build directory". O1 Bound (PLAN:104-113) and Risks do not mention this class (confidence: 60).
- **D3 [should-fix]** O3 (PLAN:156-161) says an advisory result shows "as a warning", but is silent on the failure trailer. `printSummary` prints `push gate failed: <name> — log:` for every task with status failed or interrupted (`run-push-gates.mjs:983-987`), and `firstFailure` (`:1132`, `:1290`) scans all `stepResults`.
  - A trailer for an advisory step next to exit 0 gives a contradictory signal.
  - The runner tests (PLAN:219-224) assert the exit code and a "warning", not that the trailer is absent (confidence: 45).

## (3) Blockers anywhere

Only I33 above. No named symbol is missing. The P0 consumers are exactly `run-push-gates.mjs:1144` and `:1157`. `check-gate-routing.mjs` routes `pnpm rust:target:evict` through `routeCommands`, so the `check-gate-routing.mjs` entry in Phase 1 Files stays conditional. `d-20260930-03` and `d-20261002-01` concern worker sizing and placement, not P0 order.

## Named limitations

- I could not check whether the gate runs inside a PID namespace or a `hidepid` `/proc` mount. If the step runs in a sandbox with its own PID namespace, other checkouts' processes would be invisible to the in-use test. E5's 91 same-user plus 341 other-user processes suggest a full host view when measured (confidence: 35).
- I could not run cargo or `flock` probes, so E1 and E2 are taken as recorded.

## LATE OBSERVATIONS

- [should-fix] PLAN:286-287 — "roughly once every few weeks per long-lived checkout" has no stated basis. The mandate's growth figures (6.9 to 14.1 GB per active day) put a 15 GB headroom at days to a week or so. Each trip evicts the whole `debug` unit, which is the largest (confidence: 40).
- [nit] PLAN:139-141 — `/proc/<pid>/exe` of a process that is mid-exit may return ESRCH. That is neither EACCES nor ENOENT, so it would count as an undetermined skip and a sporadic exit 1 (confidence: 30).

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-plan-r4.txt

Round 4: no blockers, and I'm giving APPROVED. Two of the open issues (I39, I55) are not closed, and I found three new should-fix defects. I could not read `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (rules 4, 6d, 12b) because it sits outside the allowed directories, so the plan's "rule 6d" rejection of a timer is unverified. I read `gate-tooling.md` and `async-resource-invariants.md` from this worktree, which is at BASE 385287ef. I ran no probes.

## (1) Open issues

- I24 CLOSED. O1 "Bound" now holds only directly after the step, and the one-week observation reads the roots at that point. The wording left behind in Goal is a new finding in section 2.
- I33 CLOSED. O6 now has a symlinked-ancestor case and a name-prefix sibling case. A stub that compares spelled paths or uses a raw prefix match would fail both.

### lens-platform-semantics-r1.txt

Platform review of the plan at `tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md`. I read the plan and the repo's CI workflow and scripts. I could not read the `/tmp/.../plan-r1.md` copy because of the sandbox, so I used the committed plan file, which I assume has the same content.

Linux is the only supported runtime, and the plan is explicit about that. CI runs `pnpm gates:contract:check`, which would run the new `*-tests.mjs`, only in the ubuntu `test` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:162,206`). `rust-macos-test` and `rust-windows-test` never execute these scripts. The Windows cross-target build directory (`target/x86_64-pc-windows-gnu/debug`) is within the three-level search depth. The remaining platform risks are below.

[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:108 — The "non-Linux exits 0" claim has no stated detection rule and no proof. "A host without Linux `flock`" could mean probing for the binary, and macOS can have a Homebrew `flock` whose flags (`-n`, `-E`) differ from util-linux. A probe-by-binary could then run eviction on macOS with different lock semantics. Gate on `process.platform === "linux"` first, as `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs:421` does, and make the platform injectable so the O6 test can exercise the non-Linux branch. The plan should also say that the ubuntu `test` job is the only place these tests run, so the macOS and Windows no-op path is proven by injection and not by CI. (confidence: 50)

[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:92 — The plan doesn't say how a `flock` is held while the Node process deletes entries. Node has no `flock`, so the only option is a `flock(1)` child holding each lock. If the gate runner is killed (timeout, SIGKILL, closed terminal), an orphaned holder keeps the `.cargo-lock` inodes locked. Every later cargo in that checkout would then block indefinitely. Linux has no default parent-death signal, and other Unixes differ again. The plan should require the holder to exit when its stdin pipe closes, or to run under a bounded lifetime. O6 should test that killing the parent releases the lock. (confidence: 55)

[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:80 — The oversize predicate uses "apparent size", but the 32 GiB threshold is calibrated against `du` figures (17 GB for `debug`). `du` counts each hard-linked inode once. A Node walk that sums `st_size` per path counts hard links repeatedly. Cargo's incremental session directories and its `deps`-to-`debug/` artifact links use hard links. The plan should dedupe by `dev:ino` (`nlink > 1`) and say so. Without that, the threshold is not comparable to the measurement it cites. A fresh `debug` could land near 32 GiB and be evicted every run, which contradicts "a normally warm unit never trips it". (confidence: 55)

[nit] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:78 — The idle signal assumes "every compile" updates the unit directory and its direct `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental` children, but the probe only shows that for an actual compile. A fully fresh cargo run (no source change, so gates are warm) does not create new entries there. Cargo only touches `invoked.timestamp` under `-Zmtime-on-use`, and that file is a grandchild anyway. A checkout that is gated daily without source changes, such as main after a no-op pull, would still be evicted after 7 days. The plan should either measure a no-op `cargo clippy` and `cargo test` and confirm what is written, or accept that case. The bound itself is unaffected. (confidence: 40)

Claims I checked and found sound:
- The `noatime` argument, the choice of mtime, and the in-place emptying that keeps lock inodes are consistent with the stated Linux probe.
- The symlink and cross-filesystem guards are adequate.
- The mutation-cache fence is correctly keyed to `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress`.
- `check-gate-routing.mjs` is not affected by platform.

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-platform-semantics-r2.txt

Bash was denied in this session, so I read the plan, the delta, the CI workflow and the runner with the file tools only. I compared the plan with the repository; I ran no commands and no probes.

**(1) Open issues**

- I16 CLOSED: O2 "Platform" (plan body lines 130–131) gates on an injectable `process.platform === "linux"` and prints one skipped line elsewhere. Windows and macOS therefore never reach `flock` or `/proc`. The node test files run only in the ubuntu `test` job (`.github/workflows/test.yml:206`). The Windows and macOS jobs run `cargo` only. No new Rust code is touched, so `rust-windows-test`, `rust-macos-test` and `pnpm rust:windows:check` are not the proof here. The r1 sentence about a Linux host without `flock` was dropped. The generic "ambiguous observation skips" rule covers a spawn ENOENT only if the executor reads it that way.
- I17 CLOSED: the remover runs inside nested `flock -n` holders (lines 108–112), so the lock lives on the open file description for as long as the remover lives. `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:678` spawns with `detached: true`. `scripts/child-supervisor.mjs:36` signals `-child.pid`, so a signal reaches the whole group. A killed wrapper cannot leave a lock held after the remover ends. A remover killed halfway leaves a fingerprint-less unit, which cargo rebuilds.
- I18 CLOSED: line 76 counts each device and inode once and O6 tests a hard-linked file counted once. The metric wording is a nit under LATE OBSERVATIONS.
- [should-fix] I12 NOT CLOSED: the correction rests on the `/proc/<pid>/cwd` guard (lines 86–90, 125–128), and that guard breaks on realistic Linux. See the first two defects below.

**(2) Defects in delta hunks**

- [should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:127 — "an unreadable `/proc` entry for a foreign checkout" skips the unit. On Linux a non-root user gets EACCES from `readlink /proc/<pid>/cwd` for any process of another uid. Root-owned daemons and kernel threads are always present, and `hidepid` mounts widen this. Read literally, every foreign unit is skipped on every run, so idle eviction never fires and the bound is never met.
  - A process that exits between `readdir` and `readlink` gives ENOENT. Read literally, that is also "unreadable", so skips would be flaky.
  - The plan must say which `/proc` failures count as ambiguous. A reasonable split is: only entries of the current uid matter, other-uid EACCES is ignored, and ENOENT means the process is gone.
  - The guard also sees only the runner's own pid namespace. A sandboxed or containerised gate run sees no other checkout's processes, so the check passes vacuously. Name that as a known limit in Risks (confidence: 70).
- [should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:88 — the check compares a spelled path with a kernel-resolved one. `/proc/<pid>/cwd` is the canonical path. The checkout path comes from `git worktree list --porcelain`, which is whatever was recorded. If a checkout is reached through a symlinked or bind-mounted ancestor, the prefix test never matches. The guard then fails open and evicts under a live gate, which contradicts line 125's "fail direction is skip". This is the same spelling-versus-binding class as `f-20260912-05` and `f-20260919-05`.
  - The plan should require comparing canonical paths on both sides, or comparing by device and inode.
  - The O6 live-process case (lines 174–175) uses `os.tmpdir()`, which cannot catch this. It needs a fixture checkout reached through a symlink.
  - The prefix test must also respect path boundaries, so that `.../chessfable` does not match `.../chessfable.worktrees` (confidence: 55).

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

### lens-platform-semantics-r3.txt

Plan r3 is not ready: I33 stays open as a should-fix, and I21, I12 and I23 are closed. I could not run `flock`, read `/proc/mounts`, or `cd` into the plan's own worktree here. So the `flock` and `/proc` claims below come from what I know of kernel and util-linux behavior, not from a local probe. Plan lines are cited against `…/tasks/plans/.plan-2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md-r3.body.md`, shortened to `plan-r3:<line>`.

## (1) Open issues

- I21 CLOSED. Plan-r3:136-142 reads `/proc/<pid>/exe` only for same-user processes and ignores EACCES and ENOENT. Any other error, or a failure to list `/proc`, is an undetermined skip that exits 1.
  - On Linux, a process that exits mid-scan gives ENOENT. When the task is already gone, `proc_fd_access_allowed` returns false, which gives EACCES. A zombie gives ENOENT. All three are ignored safely.
  - The seven unreadable-exe processes in E5 (plan 414) are non-dumpable daemons, so ignoring EACCES does not open a hole for them.
- I12 CLOSED. Plan-r3:97-102 and 80-87 move the "fresh cargo run writes nothing" gap (E2) onto the in-use test. A running test binary is caught by its executable, which sits in `deps` while cargo has freed its locks (E1). The remaining gap, a reader that runs no executable from the unit, is stated as a known limit at plan-r3:293-295.
- I23 CLOSED. Plan-r3:76-78 specifies allocated blocks with each inode counted once, which matches `du -sh`. Two small points, both in LATE OBSERVATIONS.
- [should-fix] I33 NOT CLOSED — plan-r3:143, plan-r3:188-218. The mechanism is only the blanket sentence "Every path comparison uses canonical paths on both sides". It names none of the three comparisons it governs:
  - the own checkout against the `git worktree list` entries;
  - `/proc/<pid>/exe` against the unit;
  - the "never follow a symlinked root or unit" check, which `realpath` would otherwise erase.

  O6 has no case where the spelling differs from the canonical path. The "In use" fixture (plan-r3:195) lives in `os.tmpdir()`, which is already canonical on Linux. A stub that compares spelled paths would therefore pass every O6 assertion. That is the shape of `f-20260912-05` and `f-20260919-05`, where canonical-only fixtures hid the spelling mismatch.

  Needed:
  - O2 states that the symlink `lstat` on the root and each unit happens before any `realpath` of ancestors.
  - O6 adds a case where the checkout is reached through a symlinked ancestor spelling. In that case the own checkout is still recognised and the cap still applies. A process whose executable is in a unit under that spelling still keeps it. A symlinked root or unit is still not followed. It must fail against a spelled-path stub. (confidence: 70)

## (2) Defects introduced by the delta

- [nit] plan-r3:82 — "resolved against the unit's canonical path" does not say the comparison is segment-aware. A plain string prefix would treat `…/target/debug-x/…` as inside `…/target/debug`. That errs toward keeping a unit, so it is not unsafe. (confidence: 40)
- [nit] plan-r3:80-87 — "same-user" is not defined. It should be the uid of `/proc/<pid>` against `process.getuid()`, not an `exe` read that succeeds. A stat error other than ENOENT should be an undetermined skip, like the `exe` read. (confidence: 35)

The new test script is chained into `gates:contract:check`, and `.github/workflows/test.yml:206` runs that only on `ubuntu-latest` (job `test`). So the flock and `/proc` tests are not an Actions regression on macOS or Windows.

## (3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None found.

- [nit] plan-r3:188-190 — the `git init` plus `git worktree add` fixture needs an initial commit, so it needs `-c user.name/user.email`. A CI runner has no git identity. (confidence: 35)
