### lens-tauri-security-r1.txt

Lens-scoped review of plan r1 (f-20261017-04). I found one `should-fix` defect in the recursive-deletion safety. I could not test it empirically, because Bash was denied in this session.

The plan is a local build-cache eviction script. It does not touch credentials, session storage, signed-download manifests, or the data returned to the renderer, so those lens classes do not occur here. The threat model names concurrency, not malice, as the adversary, and I held the plan to that. What is in scope is recursive deletion of other checkouts' directories (O2) and the fence and lock gating around it.

[should-fix] tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md:90-101 (O2), :48-53 (probe premise) — The safety claim "a lock that is already held means cargo is running, otherwise it is safe to empty the unit in place" rests on a probe of the compile phase only. The probe used a `build.rs` that sleeps 25 s.

- **Where the premise fails:** the build-directory lock is not held while built binaries execute. Cargo drops it when `compile` returns, before `cargo test`/`cargo run` start the binaries. A test or coverage run therefore spends most of its wall time with no lock held.
- **Repo caller that reads after the lock is gone:** `scripts/rust-branch-coverage.mjs:204-205` reads executables and `.profraw` from `src-tauri/target/llvm-cov-target/debug/deps` after cargo exits.
- **Failure case:** a gate in checkout A is in its test or coverage phase. A gate in checkout B (a side lane, the drain or Felix by hand) runs the P0 step. It finds A's `debug` or `llvm-cov-target/debug` unit over 32 GiB and takes the lock, because nothing holds it. It then deletes `deps/*` while the test binaries are being exec'd or the report is being read, so A fails.
- **Second window:** O1 decides idle or oversize before the lock is taken, and O2 does not say the predicate is evaluated again under the lock. A build that finishes between selection and lock acquisition leaves a fresh unit looking evictable.
- **Mitigation the plan already has:** the mutation fence covers the same gap for `mutants.out`. Nothing equivalent covers `src-tauri/target`.
- **Fix at plan level:** (a) re-read the mtime predicate after acquiring the locks and abort the unit if it is now fresh. (b) Require a short recent-write grace window even for the oversize predicate. (c) Add a test where a unit is touched after selection but before lock acquisition and must be skipped, and name that residual window in O2. The existing O6 tests all use a held lock or a static fixture, so none of them would catch this.

(confidence: 80)

Checked and found sound:
- **Mutation fence:** `prepareCache` runs before the fence is published (`run-backend-mutation.mjs:579-582`). In that window the cache is empty or idle and cargo has not started. Evicting then is harmless, and the fence covers the rest of the run, including the cargo-free gaps between cargo-mutants steps.
- **Symlinks:** the plan skips symlinked roots and units, matching `prepareCache`'s ancestor rule at `run-backend-mutation.mjs:358-367`.
- **Source files:** only directories that directly contain `.cargo-lock` are emptied, and no tracked or untracked source file is touched.
- **Interrupted eviction:** an interrupted eviction leaves a valid, smaller cargo directory, so no recovery step is needed.

Not reported (below the confidence bar or outside the threat model):
- `existsSync` on the fence fails open on a stat error.
- "Does not cross filesystems" has no mechanism or test in Node.
- Symlinked ancestors above `src-tauri/target`.
- A retained fence from a crashed mutation run blocks eviction. That fails safe but leaves the bound unmet.

VERDICT: REVISE

### lens-tauri-security-r2.txt

(1) Open issues

- I4 CLOSED. O1 now applies only the 40 GiB size cap to the gating checkout. Every other checkout gets the 7-day idle rule plus the `/proc/<pid>/cwd` check (plan r2 lines 81-95). That covers E1, where cargo frees its locks during test execution. O2 re-evaluates the rule after the locks are taken and runs the remover inside the nested `flock -n` holders. A cargo that starts later blocks on the kept lock inodes. A cargo that is compiling holds the lock, so the unit is skipped. Test-phase processes are caught by the cwd check on the re-check. The mutation cache is the exception: `run-backend-mutation.mjs:462,629` runs cargo with `CARGO_TARGET_DIR=cache` and `cwd: snapshot`, which is outside the checkout. The cwd check cannot see it. The fence does protect it, because it is published at `:582`, before any child starts, and cleared only by the finaliser. The plan's "fence-check error other than absent → skip" is therefore the right guard for that cache.

(2) Defects introduced by the delta

- [should-fix] plan r2:125-127 — The fail-direction bullet says "an unreadable `/proc` entry for a foreign checkout" skips the unit. As written, that covers every `/proc/<pid>/cwd` readlink that fails.
  - A normal workstation has root and other-uid processes whose `cwd` readlink returns EACCES. Processes that exit between the directory listing and the readlink return ENOENT or ESRCH.
  - Read literally, this skips every foreign unit on every run. The idle rule would never evict, and the one-week bound would not hold.
  - The plan should say which errors are ignorable and which skip. Other-uid, exited and kernel-thread entries are ignorable. A same-uid entry that cannot be read skips the unit.
  - (confidence: 72)
- [should-fix] plan r2:81-85 — The own checkout is exempt from the cwd check because the gating process itself has its cwd there. The size cap also fires in a checkout where cargo may be in a test phase with its locks free (E1).
  - Examples are a second `agent-gate` slot (E3) or a by-hand `cargo test` in the same checkout. The threat model names both ("Felix by hand", "a leaf").
  - Largest-first eviction would remove `target/debug/deps` under a running test. Tests that exec a sibling binary by path then fail. The plan rejects the cap for other checkouts for exactly this reason.
  - The fix is to apply the cwd check to the own checkout while excluding the evictor's own process ancestry, or to state this as a known limit in "Risks".
  - It only fires when a root is over 40 GiB, so the failure is a transient gate failure, not data loss.
  - (confidence: 70)

(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan

None. The other points in this lens hold:
- Symlinks are never followed, and inner symlinks are removed as links.
- `.fingerprint` is removed first, so a partial removal leaves nothing cargo trusts.
- Lock files and their inodes survive.
- Ambiguous observations skip.
- The advisory step cannot swallow `mutation-guard` or `setup-rust` failures, given the name-lookup test.

LATE OBSERVATIONS

- None. The credential, session, signed-download and renderer-diagnostic classes are not touched by this plan.

VERDICT: APPROVED

### lens-tauri-security-r3.txt

I've checked the two open issues against the plan, the delta and the repo. I'm writing the report now.

**(1) Open issues**

- I4 CLOSED. The in-use test (held lock, or a same-user process whose `/proc/<pid>/exe` lies in the unit) now gates both rules. It is re-checked under the lock (O2). It covers the test phase that E1 showed runs with the locks free, and the remaining windows are stated as known limits (Risks).
- I21 CLOSED. The cwd check is gone. EACCES and ENOENT on `exe` are ignored, any other `/proc` error is an undetermined skip, and that skip exits 1 (O2, O3). A real host no longer skips every foreign unit. One premise under it is wrong; see the first LATE OBSERVATIONS item. I don't count it against closure.

**(2) Defects introduced by the delta**

None at blocker or should-fix level.

I checked the delta hunks against this lens's mutation and containment concerns:
- The remover runs inside nested `flock -n` holders, so the locks end with it on every exit path.
- Lock files and their inodes stay, so no lock splits across inodes.
- `.fingerprint` goes first, with a stop rule if it can't be fully removed.
- Symlinks inside a unit are removed as links and never followed.
- Symlinked roots and units are skipped.
- Path comparisons are canonical on both sides.
- The mutation fence skips the mutation cache.
- Anything undetermined skips rather than evicts and now exits 1.

The adversary is concurrency, not malice (Threat model), so a path swap between selection and removal is out of scope.

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [nit] `.plan r3 O1 "In use"` — The text says an unreadable same-user `exe` "cannot be a cargo-built binary" (E5). The repo disproves that. `src-tauri/src/db/encoding.rs:604` calls `prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)` inside a test binary. `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs:41` sets the env var that triggers it. That binary is cargo-built, runs from `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target/.../deps`, and has an unreadable `exe` (EACCES), so the in-use test would ignore it. The `.mutation-in-progress` fence, held for the whole run, covers it today, so no unit is exposed now. Narrow the claim to "none of the E5 processes is cargo-built". Say that the fence, not the `exe` test, protects non-dumpable mutation test binaries. A stale fence skips forever, which fails safe. (confidence: 88)
- [nit] `.plan r3 O2 "Re-check under the lock"` — The re-check lists the rule and the in-use test, not the fence. Cargo would block on the held lock if a mutation run started in that window, and the unit would then be rebuilt. This is safe, but one clause saying the fence is re-read under the lock would remove the ambiguity. (confidence: 80)

Limitation, below the reporting threshold: I could not confirm whether the gate's `cargo test` runs doctests. Doctest binaries run from a temp directory, so neither the lock nor the `exe` test would see them. If doctests run, the "Between two test binaries" known limit should name them. I did not report it as a finding.

None of this lens's other classes applies to the plan. It has no token, credential, signed-download or renderer-diagnostic surface, and it adds no refresh flow.

### lens-tests-r1.txt

Plan review r1, tests lens. Two limits on what I could check: Bash was denied here, and the file tools only reach the working directory and `tasks/plans`. I read the plan from `tasks/plans/2026-10-31-rust-target-eviction.md`, assuming it matches the `/tmp/.../plan-r1.md` you named. I read the rule files from this worktree's copies at the same BASE. I could not read `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or global CLAUDE.md rules 4, 6d and 12b, so I did not judge against them.

None of the new files exist yet. I checked the planned assertions against `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` and `scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs` as they stand.

[blocker] plan O6 / O3 — The plan says the step "never turns a gate red", but its only test for that is script-level (exit 0 on a failed unit, exit 0 without `flock`). In the runner, `runSerialStep` pushes every step into `stepResults`, and `firstFailure(stepResults)` sets the exit code (`run-push-gates.mjs:1050-1056`, `:1132-1133`). The P0 steps are also addressed by index (`PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0[0]` and `p0[1]`, `:1144`, `:1157`). Nothing in the plan makes the runner tolerate a failed eviction step. No runner-level test is named for a nonzero exit or a spawn error from `pnpm rust:target:evict`. Today's pattern is that a failing P0 step stops the run with its exit code (`run-push-gates-tests.mjs:1060-1076`). A wiring that inserts the step as an ordinary P0 step passes every proposed test and still lets a malformed or missing eviction command fail or skip every Rust lane. Required: a runner test where the step exits nonzero and where it fails to spawn, asserting that `setup-rust` and the lanes still run and the overall exit stays 0. A second test should show that the index lookups for `setup-rust` and `mutation-guard` still resolve the right entries. (confidence: 85)

[blocker] plan O6 / O1 — No assertion covers discovering target roots from `git worktree list --porcelain`. The mandate is "every checkout", including the reused `drain` worktree and the main checkout. A mutant that evicts only the current checkout's `src-tauri/target` passes every listed test, because they all use temp-dir fixtures. Required: a fixture with a real `git init` plus `git worktree add`, run from one checkout, asserting that stale units in the sibling and in the main checkout are evicted. Also cover a prunable or missing worktree path, which must not throw. (confidence: 82)

[should-fix] plan O6 / O2 — The "held lock skips the unit" test does not prove the two things that make the design safe.
- **Which lock files:** O2 holds all three lock files. A test that holds only `.cargo-lock` passes a mutant that checks only that file. Required: one skip case per file (`.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock`, `.cargo-artifact-lock`), each with the unit contents intact.
- **Held during removal:** the plan rejects rename-then-delete and requires removal "while held". An implementation that probes `flock -n`, releases, then deletes has a check-then-act gap and passes every listed test. Required: a seam such as an injected remove function that tries `flock -n -E 75` on each lock file mid-removal and expects 75.
- **Inode survival:** "lock files survive" must compare inodes before and after. An existence check passes a delete-and-recreate. (confidence: 82)

[should-fix] plan O6 / O1 — The idle predicate takes the newest mtime across the unit directory and its `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental` children. The plan lists only "idle and oversize selected, fresh small unit kept". If the fixture backdates everything, a mutant that reads only the unit directory's own mtime passes. Required: fixtures where only one child is fresh (kept) and where all are old (evicted), plus a boundary just under and just over 7 days. For oversize, a sparse file gives a real apparent size over 32 GiB cheaply. A check on the shipped constants would stop a silently edited bound from passing. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] plan O1 premise — The probe shows that a compile moves the mtimes. A fully fresh cargo invocation, or a gate satisfied from receipts with no compile, may write nothing. Then a checkout used daily would still read as idle after 7 days and be evicted. No planned check observes mtimes across a no-op `cargo build` or `cargo clippy`. Required: a probe-crate run (build twice, compare mtimes) recorded in the plan, or the predicate corrected. (confidence: 70, a named limitation rather than a confirmed hole)

[should-fix] plan O6 / O2 — O2 promises no symlink-following inside a unit and no crossing of filesystems. O6 tests only a symlinked root or unit. A walker that uses `stat` instead of `lstat` on an entry inside the unit passes. Required: a unit containing a symlink to an external directory, asserting the external directory and its files are untouched. The filesystem-boundary claim has no test and needs either one or an explicit "untested" note. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] plan O4 / O6 — Pre-review mode has no P0 stage today (`PRE_REVIEW_GATE_SCHEDULE` has `p1` and `lanes` only). The plan wants the step "before the pre-review Rust compilation" but names no trigger and no assertion. Required: tests that the step runs before `windows-clippy` only when that lane is selected, and does not run in a docs-only pre-review. Another test should show ordering before `setup-rust` in `--rust` and `--bindings` runs and absence in a contract-only run. "Accept the new P0 step" would be satisfied by merely updating `scheduledFakeCommands`. (confidence: 80)

[should-fix] plan O6 — Verification is a synthetic `flock` child plus timing runs. No test or recorded run has the real eviction script running against a real cargo build holding its locks. The probe checked `flock` exit codes, not the script. The mandate's proof is `du -sh` against a bound. The plan defers that to "next-finding §5" and has no `du -sh` of the real checkouts before and after the first real run. That first run is feasible now: the `side-lane-codex-20261022` and `side-lane-*-20261023` worktrees are more than 7 days idle. Required: one recorded run of the eviction command, with its per-unit lines and `du -sh` before and after on the real checkouts, plus one run concurrent with a real long cargo build showing the unit is skipped and the build completes. (confidence: 78)

[nit] plan O6 — The timing measurement is a single sample of each of three runs, and the warm run after the baseline mostly hits receipts. It supports "step overhead is small" but not "warm gate times stay within baseline". The failure-isolation test also needs to skip itself when run as root, where an unremovable entry cannot be made. (confidence: 60)

### lens-tests-r2.txt

I'm wrapping up the round-2 re-review. I've read the plan, the delta and the runner and routing sources. My Bash was limited to this worktree, so the global `~/.claude` files weren't readable.

**(1) Open issues**

- I1 CLOSED. O6 now has runner tests for a non-zero exit, a spawn failure and a crash of the step. In each, `setup-rust` and every lane still run and the exit code stays the other steps'. Without the advisory marking, `firstFailure` (`scripts/run-push-gates.mjs:1290`) would pick the step up and the exit-code assertion would go red. The harness has no per-command spawn failure today (`omittedExecutables` removes `pnpm` for every step), so the executor must add one.
- I2 CLOSED. The tests assert order (after `mutation-guard`, before `setup-rust`) with `--rust` and `--bindings`, and assert the step is absent in contract-only and frontend-only runs. They also assert that `setup-rust` and `mutation-guard` resolve to their own commands, and that pre-review is unchanged. A leftover `p0[1]` would run eviction as `setup-rust` and fail the command-identity assertion. The explicit assertions matter, because existing expectations are built from the schedule itself (`scheduledFakeCommands`) and would stay green.
- I9 CLOSED. A real `git worktree add` with a sibling and the main checkout is specified.
- I10 CLOSED. It has a skip case per lock file, a `flock -n -E 75` seam during removal, and inode preservation. The new killed-step case is addressed in (2).
- I11 CLOSED for the original gap. A mutant that reads only the unit's own mtime now fails the "one fresh child" case. A residual per-child gap is in (2).
- I12 CLOSED. A live-cwd keep case and "own checkout never idle-evicted" are both specified. The `/proc`-unreadable branch is still untested; see (2).
- I13 CLOSED. A symlink inside a unit is removed as a link and the external files stay, and symlinked roots and units are not followed.
- [should-fix] I14 NOT CLOSED. The `du` before and after evidence is fine. The "concurrent with a real long cargo build" run cannot show the lock works. A building checkout is written continuously, so its units have fresh mtimes and a cwd inside, and the idle rule and the cwd check skip it before any lock is tried. A stub that ignores the lock produces the same output. The evidence has to show the skip reason is "lock held". That needs a path where only the lock protects, such as the own-checkout size-cap path with the root forced over the cap by sparse files while a real cargo holds the lock. (confidence: 85)

**(2) Defects in the delta, or in obligations that depend on it**

- [should-fix] `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:18` and `:888` — The live-repo test pins `gates:contract:check` to the verbatim `CONTRACT_CHAIN` string. Chaining `rust:target:evict:test` into it, as Phase 1 requires, turns `pnpm gates:routing:test` red until this file is updated. The Phase 1 file list does not name it; it lists `check-gate-routing.mjs` only "if its route table needs" it. This is necessary wiring work that the plan omits. The PROOF COMMAND would catch it late. (confidence: 88)
- [should-fix] O2 "Fail direction is skip" — Five branches are named, and the plan has a deletion assertion for none of them. They are a `flock` exit other than 0 or 75, a failed `stat` or size walk, a fence-check error other than "absent", an unreadable `/proc` entry, and `git worktree list` failing. An implementation that treats any `flock` exit other than 75 as acquired, or treats a fence read error as "absent", passes every listed test and deletes a live unit. O6 lists only "missing or prunable listed path does not throw". Each branch needs a case asserting the unit's contents are intact. (confidence: 82)
- [should-fix] O2 "Order" and the killed-step case in O6 — Nothing asserts `.fingerprint` is removed first. The wording "no lock stays held once its remover has ended" is true for any implementation, because the OS releases a flock when the process exits, so it checks nothing. A meaningful anchor is: force the removal to stop part-way (an unremovable later entry, or a kill) and assert `.fingerprint` is gone while other entries remain. A reverse-order implementation would leave fingerprints pointing at deleted artifacts and pass today's suite. (confidence: 80)
- [should-fix] O1 idle rule, O6 "only one fresh child" — The wording describes one case. The unit mtime is checked as well, and the four direct children are `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental`. A mutant that drops one child from the newest-mtime scan survives unless there is one fresh-child case per child. (confidence: 80)
- [should-fix] O1 "Bound" — "Non-unit content (for example `target/doc`) counts toward the 40 GiB and is reported, not removed" has no test. A mutant that totals units only, or one that removes non-unit content, passes every listed case. The cap-unreachable case (non-unit content alone over 40 GiB) has no specified behavior and no test. (confidence: 80)

**(3) Blockers anywhere in the plan**

None. The central behaviors each have a named assertion that fails on revert. These are the idle rule, the size cap, lock-held removal, the advisory step, and the by-name P0 lookup.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [nit] I could not read `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (rules 4, 6d, 12b) because the file tools are confined to the worktree. The judgements above rest on the two repo rule files and the plan's own text.

### lens-tests-r3.txt

Review of the r3 plan against the verification lens. I could not read `~/.claude/references/review-lens-contract.md` or `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (rules 4, 6d, 12b). Both were outside the sandbox or denied. I judged against the two named repo rule files and the source at BASE `385287ef`.

**(1) Open issues**

- I14 CLOSED — O6 now runs the exported function against a real probe crate: `build.rs` sleeping under `cargo build` for the lock, and a sleeping test under `cargo test` for the executable. The two outcomes carry different labels. A lock-ignoring stub would report "executable" during the build, because the running build-script binary also lies in `build/`. The label check therefore works only if the lock test is evaluated before the exe test. O1 doesn't say so.
- I28 CLOSED — `check-gate-routing-tests.mjs:888` asserts `scripts["gates:contract:check"] === CONTRACT_CHAIN`, and `:896` checks each member. Both go red if `package.json` and the pin drift apart. Phase 1 Files now lists the pin.
- [blocker] I29 NOT CLOSED — O6 "Fail direction" covers only the skip direction. The ignore branches the round-2 change introduced have no assertion: EACCES on a same-user `exe` is ignored, and so is ENOENT. E5 shows seven such EACCES processes on this machine. Revert test: treat every `readlink` error as undetermined. Every listed test stays green. On this machine, every run would then skip every unit and exit 1, so nothing is ever evicted. Also unspecified: the failing-`git worktree list` case should assert that the own checkout is still processed. A stub that aborts everything on a listing failure would pass as written. (confidence: 82)
- I30 CLOSED — "unremovable entry inside `.fingerprint` leaves every other entry intact" goes red for any others-first removal. "Second-pass failure leaves `.fingerprint` gone" goes red for a fingerprint-last stub that stops at the first failure.
- I31 CLOSED — one case per signal (unit directory, `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build`, `incremental`) with only that one fresh. Dropping any one child from the scan turns its case red.
- I32 CLOSED for non-unit content and the unreachable cap, with exit 1. Residual: O1 says in-use units alone exceeding the cap is also reported as a warning, and no case covers that path.

**(2) Defects in the delta or in obligations that depend on it**

- [should-fix] O6 Runner tests — The O3 delta says an interrupt of the run and a `childCleanup.error` latch from a failed advisory-step termination keep their existing handling. No assertion covers either. The runner tests cover advisory non-zero exit, spawn failure and crash only.
  - The existing signal table (`run-push-gates-tests.mjs:1216`, `:1231`) has no eviction-step row.
  - Revert test: an advisory implementation that swallows a termination failure or a signal passes everything listed.
  - That latch is the only protection against a live lock holder left behind.
  - Add a signal-during-eviction case and a termination-failure case that still blocks later starts and fails the run. (confidence: 80)
- [should-fix] O6 Eviction unit tests, Locks and Safety — The skip cases (one per lock file, mutation fence, symlink) don't say the fixture is otherwise evictable, or that the reason line is asserted.
  - If the fixture is fresh or under the cap, it is skipped as "not idle" or "under the cap". A lock-ignoring or fence-ignoring stub then stays green.
  - The opening claim, "must go red against a stub that … ignores the lock", needs each skip case paired with an eligible control.
  - Only the in-use case has one. (confidence: 80)
- [should-fix] O6 Size cap — Only the own-checkout cap is asserted, plus "own checkout never idle-evicted". Nothing asserts that an oversize but recently written unit in another checkout is kept. That is the rejected design, because cargo frees its locks while tests run. Revert test: apply the cap to every root. The Discovery test, where stale units in the sibling are evicted, stays green. (confidence: 82)
- [should-fix] O2 Re-check and O6 Re-check — The delta added the in-use test to the re-check under the lock. The only test refreshes mtime between selection and lock. Dropping the in-use re-check turns nothing red. Reuse the same seam to start an exe-in-unit process between selection and lock. (confidence: 80)
- [should-fix] O1/O2 canonical paths — Round 2 rejected the `cwd` check partly because a spelled path doesn't match the kernel's canonical path. "Every path comparison uses canonical paths on both sides" has no test. A Linux `mkdtemp` fixture is already canonical, so comparing uncanonicalised paths stays green. Add a fixture reached through a symlinked ancestor, with the exe running inside it. (confidence: 82)
- [should-fix] O6 Real-machine evidence and Timing — The central integration claim is that cargo rebuilds into an emptied unit with its lock files kept. It is never exercised through the eviction code.
  - The probe cases only show skips.
  - The cold-with-sccache run empties `target/debug` by hand.
  - A remover that leaves a broken state would pass every fixture test and every real run. Examples: partial `deps`, or a deleted lock file with a stale inode.
  - Add: after the probe's `cargo test`, run the function with a threshold that forces eviction, assert bytes freed and lock inodes unchanged, then require `cargo build` to succeed. (confidence: 82)
- [should-fix] O6 Timing — The MANDATE's proof is "warm gate timings stay within the measured baseline". The plan records one baseline, one warm-overhead run and one cold run, with CR-1 adding only sample counts. It states no pass threshold. A step that added minutes to every gate would be recorded and pass. Fix: define the tolerance (for example, overhead as a fraction of baseline, and the step's own runtime below a stated bound). (confidence: 80)

**(3) Blockers anywhere**

Only I29, above.

**LATE OBSERVATIONS**

- [nit] O6 Constants — asserting the shipped 40 GiB and 7-day values pins numbers, not behavior. It is acceptable here only because the plan states the measurement basis beside the constants.
- [nit] CR-2 (`" (deleted)"` suffix handling) is carried to diff review with no O6 case. A rebuilt binary that is still running would not match the unit and would be evicted. Confidence is below the reporting threshold, so this is listed only for tracking.

[should-fix] r4 body:104-107 / 221-223 — The idle rule now says an absent `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` or `incremental` child "is not an error". O6 has no case for it. The idle-rule cases only vary which present child is fresh. `target/release` units normally have no `incremental` directory. An implementation that treats ENOENT there as undetermined passes every listed test, yet it would report exit 1 on every gate and never evict release units. A stub that computes "unreachable" as `nonUnitBytes > cap` passes everything, and an in-use-pinned oversize root then exits 0 with no warning. Add a case: an in-use unit alone keeps the root over the cap, and the unreachable line and exit 1 are asserted. (confidence: 80)

That is the I63 false-green. At BASE, `recordChildOutcome` copies `terminationError` onto the task, and `:773` refuses later starts once `childCleanup.error` is set. O6 should therefore assert that an advisory step whose supervisor reports unconfirmed termination (a) is recorded `failed`, not `warning`, (b) makes the run exit non-zero, (c) sets `childCleanup.error`, and (d) refuses later starts. The only size cases are "allocated files push a root over a small threshold" and "a hard-linked file is counted once". If the walker reverted to `st_size`, both stay green. None. The central behaviors each have a revert-sensitive anchor in O6. These are idle eviction in other checkouts, the cap in the own checkout, locks held during removal, the in-use test and the advisory-status mapping. The new test script is chained into `gates:contract:check` and the pinned `CONTRACT_CHAIN`, so the gate actually runs it. ### plan-r1.md

# Plan: bound Rust build output in every ChessFable checkout (f-20261017-04)

## Goal

Every cargo build directory of every ChessFable checkout stays below a stated bound without
manual cleanup, while gates in the checkout that is building stay warm in the ordinary case.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261017-04:

* **Where:** `src-tauri/target/` in the main checkout and in every worktree that runs the Rust gates (`chessfable.worktrees/drain/src-tauri/target/`), plus `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target/`. Written by the gate scripts that drive cargo: `scripts/gate-receipt.mjs`, `scripts/rust-branch-coverage.mjs` / `scripts/coverage-report.mjs` (`llvm-cov-target`), `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs` (`mutants.out`). No script, gate or drain step ever removes stale artifacts.
* **Defect:** cargo keeps every artifact for every dependency version, feature set and profile it has ever built, and nothing in the project evicts them. Measured on tuxedo-atlas 2026-10-17: main checkout `src-tauri/target` 123 GB (`debug/incremental` 56 GB across 86 crate-session directories, `debug/deps` 22 GB of which 3,640 files date from August and 2,743 from September, `llvm-cov-target` 23 GB, cross targets 13 GB). `chessfable.worktrees/drain/src-tauri/target` 32 GB, all written since 2026-10-14 (17.3 GB on 10-14, 6.9 GB on 10-16, 14.1 GB on 10-17). `mutants.out` 6.9 GB, plus an 8 GB copy of a target under `/tmp/catalog-engine-phase1-20261016/snapshot`. The main target wrote 12.1 GB on 10-11 and 9.1 GB on 10-13. Together that is about 160 GB on a root filesystem that ran full on 2026-10-12 (from a different cause, agent-kit `f-20261012-10`). It has no ceiling. sccache (`~/.cache/sccache`, 5.6 GB) is already present, so a cold rebuild after eviction costs less than a cold build without it.
* **Open question:** where eviction lives and what it keeps. Options include: delete a worktree's `target/` when its build/drain run lands; evict artifacts not used for N days in long-lived targets (main checkout, the reused `drain` worktree) from a gate wrapper; cap total size; or share one target directory per profile across worktrees. The plan must weigh warm-cache gate times (measured before and after) against the bound, and decide whether the worktree half belongs here or in agent-kit's worktree lifecycle.
* **Proof:** after one week of normal drain activity with the fix in place, `du -sh` of every ChessFable `target/` and `mutants.out` stays under the chosen bound, and warm gate timings stay within the measured baseline.

## Threat model and non-goals

Inputs are this repository's own checkouts on Felix's Linux workstation. The adversary is
concurrency, not malice: another session may be running cargo in any checkout (a drain, a
side lane, a build worktree, a leaf, Felix by hand) at the moment eviction runs, and a
previous eviction may have been interrupted. Symlinked or foreign directories must never be
followed. CI runners are ephemeral and out of scope. Non-Linux hosts are out of scope beyond
not failing a gate. Deliberately evasive layouts are out of scope.

## Traced premises

Measured 2026-10-31 at BASE 385287ef:

* Current sizes: main checkout `src-tauri/target` 6.4 GB (cleaned by hand at some point),
  `chessfable.worktrees/drain` 27 GB (every entry dated 2026-10-27 or later, so it was also
  recreated by hand), `side-lane-codex-20261027` 26 GB, `side-lane-codex-20261023` 9.7 GB,
  `side-lane-astra-20261023` 8.8 GB, `side-lane-codex-20261022` 2.0 GB. No `mutants.out` in
  the main checkout today. `/` is 42 % used.
* A freshly built warm checkout weighs about 26 GB: `debug` 17 GB (`incremental` 7.5,
  `deps` 6.8, `build` 2.5), `llvm-cov-target` 6.3 GB, `release` 2.0 GB,
  `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` 1.4 GB (drain and side-lane-codex-20261027 measured identically).
* All four side-lane branches are fully merged into master and their worktrees are clean
  (`git rev-list --count master..build/<branch>` = 0, `git status --porcelain` empty). They
  were never removed, so their build output (46.5 GB) idles. `kit worktree remove`
  (agent-kit `lib/kit_worktree.py`, `remove`) already deletes a whole linked worktree,
  `target/` included, once its branch is merged and the tree is clean.
* `/` is mounted `noatime` (`mount`), so access-time based eviction (cargo-sweep `--time`
  and `--stamp`/`--file` modes) cannot tell a used artifact from an unused one. Modification
  times are reliable. Every compile creates a new `incremental/<crate>-<hash>/s-*` session and
  rewrites the `deps` directory entries of the workspace crate, which updates those
  directories' mtimes (probe: `deps` mtime moved to the build's end time).
* Cargo 1.98.0 creates `.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock` and `.cargo-artifact-lock` in each
  build directory (`target/debug`, `target/release`,
  `target/x86_64-pc-windows-gnu/debug`, `target/llvm-cov-target/debug`). Disposable probe
  under `$RUN_TMP/cargo-lock-probe` (a crate whose `build.rs` sleeps 25 s): during the build
  `flock -n -E 75 <file> true` exited 75 for all three files. After the build all three
  exited 0. `flock` is `/usr/bin/flock` (util-linux).
* sccache serves every local compile through `rustc-wrapper` in `~/.cargo/config.toml`.
  Every `kit worktree create` checkout already starts with an empty `target/`, so a cold
  build with sccache is the normal first gate in every build worktree today.
* `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` `PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0` runs `mutation-guard` (always) and
  then `setup-rust` (blocks `rust`, `bindings`) before any cargo lane. The pre-review mode
  runs `pnpm rust:windows:check` among its lanes. `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs` validates
  every schedule command against package scripts. `gates:contract:check` chains every
  `node --test scripts/*-tests.mjs` package script.
* `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs` `prepareCache` keeps cargo output in
  `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` and holds the
  `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress` fence while a mutation run is live.
* Receipts in `.gate-receipts/*.json` carry no durations. No eviction code exists:
  `git grep -n -E "cargo[- ]sweep|evict"` in `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.claude/skills/` finds none.

## Approach

### O1 — Unit, bound and predicate

The eviction unit is one cargo build directory: a directory that directly contains
`.cargo-lock`, found at most three levels below a cargo target root. The target roots are
`src-tauri/target` and `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` of every checkout of this
repository, as listed by `git worktree list --porcelain` (the main checkout included). A
unit is evicted when either holds:

* **idle:** no cargo write for 7 days, read from the newest mtime among the unit directory
  and its direct `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental` children;
* **oversize:** its apparent size exceeds 32 GiB. That is just under twice the largest fresh
  unit (`debug`, 17 GB), so a normally warm unit never trips it, while the observed
  accumulation (the main checkout's `debug` passed 80 GB) does.

The bound this establishes: when a gate run starts, every unit in every checkout is either
younger than 7 idle days and at most 32 GiB, or skipped because cargo holds it. A checkout
nobody builds in returns to empty build directories within 7 days of its last build plus
the next gate run anywhere. The constants sit beside the code with their measurement basis,
in the same style as the gate-runner constants.

### O2 — Eviction is safe against a live cargo

Each unit is evicted in place: while every Cargo lock file present in the unit
(`.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock`, `.cargo-artifact-lock`) is held with a non-blocking
`flock`, every other entry of the unit is removed and the lock files stay. A cargo that
starts meanwhile blocks on the same lock inodes and then rebuilds into the emptied
directory. A lock that is already held means cargo is running, so the unit is skipped and
reported. A `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` is skipped while
`mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress` exists. Symlinked target roots or units are
never followed or removed. Removal does not cross filesystems or follow symlinks inside the
unit. A partly removed unit is still a valid, smaller cargo directory that cargo rebuilds,
so an interrupted eviction needs no recovery step.

### O3 — Failure never turns a gate red

Eviction is housekeeping. Each unit's outcome (evicted with bytes freed, skipped with
reason, failed with error) is printed on one line. A failure on one unit is reported and the
next unit continues. The command exits 0 unless its own invocation is malformed. On a host
without Linux `flock` it prints one skipped line and exits 0. It never fails, skips or
delays a gate lane beyond its own runtime.

### O4 — Integration point

A new P0 step `rust-target-eviction` runs in `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` before
`setup-rust` whenever the run selects the `rust` or `bindings` block, and before the
pre-review Rust compilation in `--pre-review` mode. It runs as a package script that
`check-gate-routing.mjs` accepts. These two runners execute in every checkout before any
push (drain, side lane, build worktree, main checkout), so eviction runs at least as often
as Rust gates do, with no new launcher, timer or service.

### O5 — Ownership boundary with agent-kit

Removing a finished worktree is agent-kit's lifecycle (`kit worktree remove`, build Step 10)
and already deletes its `target/`. This project removes no worktree and touches no tracked
or untracked source file in any checkout. It empties only cargo build directories, which
are caches the next build regenerates. Idle eviction therefore bounds a retained worktree's
build output whatever happens to the worktree itself.

### O6 — Verification

* Unit tests (`node --test`) on temporary directory fixtures prove: selection of idle and
  oversize units, a fresh small unit kept, a held lock (a real `flock` child) skips the unit
  with its contents intact, the mutation fence skips the mutation cache, a symlinked root or
  unit is not followed, lock files survive eviction, a failure on one unit (an unremovable
  entry) is reported while the next unit is still evicted, and the non-Linux / missing
  `flock` path exits 0. Each assertion must fail against a stubbed eviction that removes
  nothing or ignores the lock.
* The routing checker and the gate-runner tests accept the new P0 step in both modes.
* Measurement in this build's own worktree: wall time of a warm
  `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` before the change (baseline), the same run again with the step
  in place and nothing to evict (warm overhead), and one run right after the step evicted
  `target/debug` (oversize forced through a test-only constant override, or idle forced by
  backdating mtimes) for the cold-with-sccache cost. All three times and the step's own
  runtime go into the closing note. The finding's one-week `du -sh` observation follows
  under next-finding §5 "Exit with an external wait" if it is still pending at release.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (full auto, technical):

* **Where eviction lives:** in the project gate runner (O4), not in a timer, not in the
  drain and not in agent-kit. Rejected: a systemd timer or cron job (new machinery outside
  the repository, rule 6d); a drain step (misses side lanes and the main checkout, which
  never run a drain).
* **What it keeps:** everything younger than 7 idle days and at most 32 GiB per build
  directory (O1). Rejected: cargo-sweep (a new tool, and its time modes depend on access
  times that the `noatime` mount never records); removing selected stale files inside
  `deps` (requires reproducing cargo's fingerprint graph); removing only `incremental`
  (leaves the stale `deps` accumulation the finding measured).
* **Shared target directory across worktrees: rejected.** The probe shows cargo holds all
  three build locks exclusively for the whole build, so the drain, side lanes and build
  worktrees, which gate in parallel, would serialize on one build directory.
  `run-backend-mutation.mjs` `prepareCache` also relies on each checkout's own target identity.
* **Worktree half:** belongs to agent-kit's worktree lifecycle, which already removes the
  whole tree (O5). No change there. The retained merged side-lane worktrees are not filed
  as a defect: they belong to Felix-authorized side-lane sessions, and nothing establishes
  they were meant to be removed. Idle eviction bounds their disk regardless.
* **Lock-held-in-place eviction instead of rename-then-delete:** renaming the unit away
  under a held lock lets a waiting cargo acquire the moved lock inode while a later cargo
  locks a fresh one at the original path. Emptying in place keeps one lock inode per path.

## Risks / open questions

* A gate that starts on an oversize unit pays one cold build with sccache. Measured in O6.
  The expected rate is roughly once every few weeks per long-lived checkout.
* Cargo may change its lock file names. A unit is recognised by `.cargo-lock`, and every
  lock file present is held, so an added lock file name is the only gap. Stated as a known
  limit in the code comment.
* `/tmp` target copies made ad hoc by earlier sessions are outside every repository root
  and out of scope.

## Not part of this task

* Removing or pruning worktrees (agent-kit lifecycle, O5).
* sccache's own cache size (sccache enforces its configured maximum itself).
* CI caches, cargo registry and git caches under `~/.cargo`.
* Any change to gate content, gate order beyond the one P0 step, receipts or coverage.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — eviction step, wiring, tests, rule text

* **Files:** new `scripts/rust-target-eviction.mjs` and `scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs`;
  `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` (P0 step in both modes); `scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`;
  `package.json` (`rust:target:evict` and `rust:target:evict:test`, the latter chained into
  `gates:contract:check`); `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs` only if its route table needs
  the new command; `.claude/rules/gate-tooling.md` (one short "Rust build output eviction"
  section stating O1–O3 and the reference to this decision).
* **Surfaces:** filesystem deletion inside other checkouts (sensitive: concurrency and
  deletion). Role `sensitive`.
* **PROOF COMMAND:** `node --test scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs && pnpm gates:push:test && pnpm gates:routing:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm checks:pre-review`, followed by the three O6 timing runs, recorded with their wall times.

## Approach

### O1 — Unit, roots and the two eviction rules

The eviction unit is one cargo build directory: a directory that directly contains
`.cargo-lock`, found at most three levels below a cargo target root. The target roots are
`src-tauri/target` and `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` of every checkout of this
repository, as listed by `git worktree list --porcelain` (the main checkout included).
Sizes count each inode once (device and inode number), as `du` does, so hard-linked
incremental and uplifted artifacts are not counted twice.

Two rules, split by which checkout a unit belongs to:

* **Own checkout** (the one running the step, about to build): the **size cap**. When a
  target root's total exceeds 40 GiB, its units are evicted largest first until the root is
  at most 40 GiB. A fresh warm root measures about 25 GiB (26 GB), so a normally warm root
  never trips it. The observed accumulation (123 GB in the main checkout) does. The idle
  rule does not apply here, because this checkout's gate is about to use its units.
* **Every other checkout:** the **idle rule**. A unit is evicted when it has had no cargo
  write for 7 days, read from the newest mtime among the unit directory and its direct
  `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental` children, and no live process has its
  working directory inside that checkout (`/proc/<pid>/cwd`). The size cap does not apply
  to other checkouts. Their own gates apply it.

The second rule's process check exists because cargo frees its build locks while test
binaries run (Reviews, evidence E1), and because a fully fresh cargo run writes nothing in
the unit (E2). A checkout gated recently without compiling therefore can look idle, but a
gate or test running there has its working directory inside the checkout.

**Bound:** at the start of every push gate with Rust work, the gating checkout's target
roots are each at most 40 GiB. Within 7 days of its last compile, plus the next Rust push
gate in any checkout, every other checkout with no live process inside holds no build
output beyond the lock files. These are the numbers the finding's one-week `du -sh`
observation is checked against. Non-unit content in a root (for example `target/doc`)
counts toward the 40 GiB and is reported, not removed. The constants sit beside the code
with their measurement basis, in the same style as the gate-runner constants. Unit tests
pass thresholds as function arguments. No runtime override exists.

### O2 — Eviction is safe against a live cargo

* **Lock held for the whole removal:** each unit is emptied in place by a remover that runs
  inside nested non-blocking `flock -n` holders on every Cargo lock file present in the unit
  (`.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock`, `.cargo-artifact-lock`). The locks therefore live
  exactly as long as the removal process and end with it on every exit path, including a
  killed parent. The holder processes are children of the step and are covered by the
  runner's existing child cleanup on signals.
* **Re-check under the lock:** the unit's rule (O1) is evaluated again after the locks are
  acquired. If the unit no longer qualifies, nothing is removed.
* **What stays:** the lock files stay with their inodes, so a cargo that starts meanwhile
  blocks on the same inodes and then rebuilds into the emptied directory.
* **Order:** `.fingerprint` is removed first, then every other entry. Any later partial
  state has no fingerprints, so cargo rebuilds everything in it. An entry that cannot be
  removed makes that unit a failure (O3), and the next unit continues.
* **Skips:** a held lock skips the unit. A `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` is skipped while
  `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress` exists. Symlinked target roots, units or
  entries inside a unit are never followed. A symlink inside a unit is removed as a link,
  never through its target.
* **Fail direction is skip:** any ambiguous observation skips the unit and reports it, never
  evicts. That covers a `flock` exit other than 0 or 75, a failed `stat` or size walk, any
  fence-check error other than "absent", and an unreadable `/proc` entry for a foreign
  checkout. When `git worktree list --porcelain` fails, only the own checkout is processed.
  A listed path that is missing or prunable is skipped.
* **Platform:** the step evicts only when `process.platform` is `linux`, with the platform
  injectable for tests. Elsewhere it prints one skipped line and exits 0.

### O3 — Advisory step: visible, never gating

* **Script outcome:** each unit's outcome (evicted with bytes freed, skipped with reason,
  failed with error) is one line. The script catches every error at top level. It exits 0
  when no unit failed and 1 when any unit failed.
* **Runner:** the runner marks the eviction schedule entry advisory. An advisory step's
  result, whether a non-zero exit, a spawn failure or a crash, never changes the run's exit
  code and never skips or blocks a later step or lane. A non-zero advisory step shows in the
  summary as a warning, with its log tail like a failed step. An interrupt of the whole run
  keeps its existing handling.

### O4 — Integration point

A new P0 step `rust-target-eviction` runs in `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` push mode, after
`mutation-guard` and before `setup-rust`, whenever the run selects the `rust` or `bindings`
block. Contract-only and frontend-only runs do not run it. Pre-review mode is unchanged: the
push gate of the same change runs the same Rust work afterwards, and pre-review repair loops
would otherwise re-walk every checkout. The runner currently addresses P0 entries by index
(`PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0[0]`, `p0[1]`), so the change makes every P0 consumer locate its
entry by name. It runs as a package script that `check-gate-routing.mjs` accepts. Every
checkout that pushes Rust changes runs this runner (drain, side lane, build worktree, main
checkout), so no new launcher, timer or service is needed.

### O5 — Ownership boundary with agent-kit

Removing a finished worktree is agent-kit's lifecycle (`kit worktree remove`, build Step 10)
and already deletes its `target/`. This project removes no worktree and touches no tracked
or untracked source file in any checkout. It empties only cargo build directories, which
are caches the next build regenerates. Idle eviction therefore bounds a retained worktree's
build output whatever happens to the worktree itself.

### O6 — Verification

Every assertion below must go red against a stub that removes nothing, ignores the lock or
evicts only the own checkout.

* **Eviction unit tests** (`node --test`, temporary fixtures):
  * Discovery uses a real `git init` plus `git worktree add`, run from one checkout. Stale
    units in the sibling and in the main checkout are evicted, and a missing or prunable
    listed path does not throw.
  * Idle rule: a unit with only one fresh child is kept, a fully backdated one is evicted,
    with cases just under and just over 7 days. A live process whose working directory is
    inside the foreign checkout keeps its unit.
  * Size cap: sparse files give a real apparent size over the cap. Largest-first eviction
    stops once the root is under the cap. A hard-linked file is counted once. The own
    checkout is never idle-evicted.
  * Locks: one skip case per lock file, each with contents intact. A seam inside removal
    asserts that `flock -n -E 75` on each lock file returns 75 while removal runs. Lock
    files keep their inode numbers. After the step is killed mid-removal, no lock stays held once its remover has ended.
  * Re-check: a unit refreshed between selection and lock acquisition is kept.
  * Safety: the mutation fence skips the mutation cache. A symlink inside a unit pointing at
    an external directory is removed as a link, and the external files stay. Symlinked roots
    and units are not followed. An unremovable entry fails that unit, and the next unit is
    still evicted. The non-Linux branch exits 0 through the injected platform.
  * Constants: the shipped cap and idle constants are asserted.
* **Runner tests** (`scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`): the step runs after `mutation-guard`
  and before `setup-rust` with `--rust` and with `--bindings`, and is absent in
  contract-only and frontend-only runs. With the step exiting non-zero, failing to spawn and
  crashing, `setup-rust` and every selected lane still run, the exit code stays that of the
  other steps, and the summary shows the warning. `setup-rust` and `mutation-guard` still
  resolve to their own commands. Pre-review command lists are unchanged.
* **Real-machine evidence:**
  * `du -sh` of every ChessFable target root and `mutants.out`, recorded before and after
    one real run of the eviction command, with its per-unit lines. At BASE, three
    side-lane worktrees exceed 7 idle days.
  * One run concurrent with a real long cargo build in another checkout, showing the unit
    is skipped and the build completes.
* **Timing, in this build's own worktree:**
  * Wall time of a warm `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` before the change (baseline).
  * The same run with the step in place and nothing to evict (warm overhead).
  * One run directly after the step evicted `target/debug` (cold-with-sccache cost). The
    cap is forced by temporarily filling the root with sparse files, removed afterwards.
  * All three times and the step's own runtime go into the closing note.
* **One-week observation:** the finding's one-week `du -sh` observation follows under
  next-finding §5 "Exit with an external wait" if it is still pending at release.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (full auto, technical):

* **Where eviction lives:** in the project push-gate runner (O4), not in a timer, not in the
  drain, not in pre-review and not in agent-kit. Rejected: a systemd timer or cron job (new
  machinery outside the repository, rule 6d); a drain step (misses side lanes and the main
  checkout, which never run a drain); pre-review wiring (repeats the walk on every repair
  round for work the push gate repeats anyway).
* **What it keeps:**
  * The gating checkout keeps at most 40 GiB per target root.
  * Other checkouts keep everything written within 7 days, and everything while a process
    works inside them (O1).
  * Rejected: cargo-sweep (a new tool whose time modes depend on access times, which the
    `noatime` mount never records); removing selected stale files inside `deps` (requires
    reproducing cargo's fingerprint graph); removing only `incremental` (leaves the stale
    `deps` accumulation the finding measured); a size cap on other checkouts (cargo frees
    its locks while tests run, so a cap there can delete a running gate's test binaries);
    a per-unit cap (a root holds four units, so the per-root total would still reach
    about 128 GiB).
* **Shared target directory across worktrees: rejected.** The probe shows cargo holds all
  three build locks exclusively for the whole compile, so the drain, side lanes and build
  worktrees, which gate in parallel on two machine-wide gate slots, would serialize on one
  build directory. `run-backend-mutation.mjs` `prepareCache` also relies on each checkout's
  own target identity.
* **Worktree half:** belongs to agent-kit's worktree lifecycle, which already removes the
  whole tree (O5). No change there. The retained merged side-lane worktrees are not filed
  as a defect: they belong to Felix-authorized side-lane sessions, and nothing establishes
  they were meant to be removed. Idle eviction bounds their disk regardless.
* **Lock-held-in-place eviction instead of rename-then-delete:** renaming the unit away
  under a held lock lets a waiting cargo acquire the moved lock inode while a later cargo
  locks a fresh one at the original path. Emptying in place keeps one lock inode per path.

## Risks / open questions

* A gate that starts on an oversize root pays one cold build with sccache. Measured in O6.
  The expected rate is roughly once every few weeks per long-lived checkout.
* Cargo may change its lock file names. A unit is recognised by `.cargo-lock`, and every
  lock file present is held, so an added lock file name is the only gap. Stated as a known
  limit in the code comment.
* A process that reads a foreign checkout's build output with its working directory
  elsewhere is not seen by the process check. Known limit, needs both 7 idle days and such
  a reader.
* A unit skipped on every run (a lock held indefinitely) is reported in each step log and
  never evicted. Known limit.
* `/tmp` target copies made ad hoc by earlier sessions are outside every repository root
  and out of scope.

## Not part of this task

* Removing or pruning worktrees (agent-kit lifecycle, O5).
* sccache's own cache size (sccache enforces its configured maximum itself).
* CI caches, cargo registry and git caches under `~/.cargo`.
* Pre-review mode, and any change to gate content, receipts or coverage. The only gate-order
  change is the one advisory P0 step.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — eviction step, wiring, tests, rule text

* **Files:**
  * New `scripts/rust-target-eviction.mjs` and `scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs`.
  * `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs`: the advisory P0 step, advisory result handling, P0 lookup
    by name.
  * `scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`.
  * `package.json`: `rust:target:evict` and `rust:target:evict:test`, the latter chained
    into `gates:contract:check`.
  * `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs`, only if its route table needs the new command.
  * `.claude/rules/gate-tooling.md`: correct the "mutation guard first (always) and
    `setup-rust`" sentence to name the eviction step. Add one short "Rust build output
    eviction" section stating O1–O3 and naming the decision id the adopter records for this
    plan.
* **Surfaces:** filesystem deletion inside other checkouts, concurrency with live cargo
  processes, and `package.json` (sensitive glob). Role `sensitive`.
* **PROOF COMMAND:** `node --test scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs && pnpm gates:push:test && pnpm gates:routing:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm checks:pre-review`, then the O6 real-machine evidence and timing runs, recorded with their outputs and wall times.

## Carried to diff review

* CR-1 — The failure-isolation test (unremovable entry) skips itself when run as root. The
  timing runs report each measurement's sample count. (from I15)
