### plan-r5.md

# Plan: bound Rust build output in every ChessFable checkout (f-20261017-04)

## Goal

Rust build output in ChessFable checkouts gets a ceiling without manual cleanup. Directly
after each Rust push gate's eviction step, the gating checkout's target roots are below a
stated cap. A checkout nobody compiles in for 7 days is emptied of build output by the next
Rust push gate anywhere. Gates in the checkout that is building stay warm in the ordinary
case.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261017-04:

* **Where:** `src-tauri/target/` in the main checkout and in every worktree that runs the Rust gates (`chessfable.worktrees/drain/src-tauri/target/`), plus `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target/`. Written by the gate scripts that drive cargo: `scripts/gate-receipt.mjs`, `scripts/rust-branch-coverage.mjs` / `scripts/coverage-report.mjs` (`llvm-cov-target`), `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs` (`mutants.out`). No script, gate or drain step ever removes stale artifacts.
* **Defect:** cargo keeps every artifact for every dependency version, feature set and profile it has ever built, and nothing in the project evicts them. Measured on tuxedo-atlas 2026-10-17: main checkout `src-tauri/target` 123 GB (`debug/incremental` 56 GB across 86 crate-session directories, `debug/deps` 22 GB of which 3,640 files date from August and 2,743 from September, `llvm-cov-target` 23 GB, cross targets 13 GB). `chessfable.worktrees/drain/src-tauri/target` 32 GB, all written since 2026-10-14 (17.3 GB on 10-14, 6.9 GB on 10-16, 14.1 GB on 10-17). `mutants.out` 6.9 GB, plus an 8 GB copy of a target under `/tmp/catalog-engine-phase1-20261016/snapshot`. The main target wrote 12.1 GB on 10-11 and 9.1 GB on 10-13. Together that is about 160 GB on a root filesystem that ran full on 2026-10-12 (from a different cause, agent-kit `f-20261012-10`). It has no ceiling. sccache (`~/.cache/sccache`, 5.6 GB) is already present, so a cold rebuild after eviction costs less than a cold build without it.
* **Open question:** where eviction lives and what it keeps. Options include: delete a worktree's `target/` when its build/drain run lands; evict artifacts not used for N days in long-lived targets (main checkout, the reused `drain` worktree) from a gate wrapper; cap total size; or share one target directory per profile across worktrees. The plan must weigh warm-cache gate times (measured before and after) against the bound, and decide whether the worktree half belongs here or in agent-kit's worktree lifecycle.
* **Proof:** after one week of normal drain activity with the fix in place, `du -sh` of every ChessFable `target/` and `mutants.out` stays under the chosen bound, and warm gate timings stay within the measured baseline.

## Threat model and non-goals

Inputs are this repository's own checkouts on Felix's Linux workstation. The adversary is
concurrency, not malice: another session may be running cargo in any checkout (a drain, a
side lane, a build worktree, a leaf, Felix by hand) at the moment eviction runs, and a
previous eviction may have been interrupted. Symlinked or foreign directories must never be
followed. CI runners are ephemeral and out of scope. Non-Linux hosts are out of scope beyond
not failing a gate. Deliberately evasive layouts are out of scope.

## Traced premises

Measured 2026-10-31 at BASE 385287ef:

* Current sizes: main checkout `src-tauri/target` 6.4 GB (cleaned by hand at some point),
  `chessfable.worktrees/drain` 27 GB (every entry dated 2026-10-27 or later, so it was also
  recreated by hand), `side-lane-codex-20261027` 26 GB, `side-lane-codex-20261023` 9.7 GB,
  `side-lane-astra-20261023` 8.8 GB, `side-lane-codex-20261022` 2.0 GB. No `mutants.out` in
  the main checkout today. `/` is 42 % used.
* A freshly built warm checkout weighs about 26 GB: `debug` 17 GB (`incremental` 7.5,
  `deps` 6.8, `build` 2.5), `llvm-cov-target` 6.3 GB, `release` 2.0 GB,
  `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` 1.4 GB (drain and side-lane-codex-20261027 measured identically).
* All four side-lane branches are fully merged into master and their worktrees are clean
  (`git rev-list --count master..build/<branch>` = 0, `git status --porcelain` empty). They
  were never removed, so their build output (46.5 GB) idles. `kit worktree remove`
  (agent-kit `lib/kit_worktree.py`, `remove`) already deletes a whole linked worktree,
  `target/` included, once its branch is merged and the tree is clean.
* `/` is mounted `noatime` (`mount`), so access-time based eviction (cargo-sweep `--time`
  and `--stamp`/`--file` modes) cannot tell a used artifact from an unused one. Modification
  times are reliable. Every compile creates a new `incremental/<crate>-<hash>/s-*` session and
  rewrites the `deps` directory entries of the workspace crate, which updates those
  directories' mtimes (probe: `deps` mtime moved to the build's end time).
* Cargo 1.98.0 creates `.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock` and `.cargo-artifact-lock` in each
  build directory (`target/debug`, `target/release`,
  `target/x86_64-pc-windows-gnu/debug`, `target/llvm-cov-target/debug`). Disposable probe
  under `$RUN_TMP/cargo-lock-probe` (a crate whose `build.rs` sleeps 25 s): during the build
  `flock -n -E 75 <file> true` exited 75 for all three files. After the build all three
  exited 0. `flock` is `/usr/bin/flock` (util-linux).
* sccache serves every local compile through `rustc-wrapper` in `~/.cargo/config.toml`.
  Every `kit worktree create` checkout already starts with an empty `target/`, so a cold
  build with sccache is the normal first gate in every build worktree today.
* `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` `PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0` runs `mutation-guard` (always) and
  then `setup-rust` (blocks `rust`, `bindings`) before any cargo lane. The pre-review mode
  runs `pnpm rust:windows:check` among its lanes. `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs` validates
  every schedule command against package scripts. `gates:contract:check` chains every
  `node --test scripts/*-tests.mjs` package script.
* `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs` `prepareCache` keeps cargo output in
  `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` and holds the
  `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress` fence while a mutation run is live.
* Receipts in `.gate-receipts/*.json` carry no durations. No eviction code exists:
  `git grep -n -E "cargo[- ]sweep|evict"` in `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.claude/skills/` finds none.

## Approach

### O1 — Unit, roots, in-use signal and the two eviction rules

The eviction unit is one cargo build directory: a directory that directly contains
`.cargo-lock`, found at most three levels below a cargo target root. The target roots are
`src-tauri/target` and `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` of every checkout of this
repository, as listed by `git worktree list --porcelain` (the main checkout included).
Size is allocated blocks of every entry (files, directories and symlinks, by `lstat`), each
inode counted once by device and inode number. That is the measure `du` reports, so
hard-linked incremental and uplifted artifacts are not counted twice and the bound is checked
with the same measure as the finding's `du -sh`.

**In use:** a unit is in use when any of its Cargo lock files is held, or when a live
process of the current user runs an executable located inside the unit. The lock test runs
first, and its result is the reported reason when both hold. The second test compares
`/proc/<pid>/exe` with the unit's canonical path, path segment by path segment. Cargo frees
its build locks while test binaries run (Reviews, evidence E1), and a running test binary is
exactly a process whose executable lies in the unit. A process counts as the current user's
when the owner uid of `/proc/<pid>` equals the step's uid. Other users' processes are not
inspected. A same-user process whose `exe` link is unreadable (EACCES) is ignored: on this
host those are non-dumpable desktop daemons (E5). The one cargo-built binary in this
repository that makes itself non-dumpable is the encoding mutation test
(`src-tauri/src/db/encoding.rs`, `prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)`), and only under the mutation
runner. That case is protected by the mutation fence (O2), not by this test. An in-use unit
is never evicted, under either rule.

Two rules, split by which checkout a unit belongs to:

* **Own checkout** (the one running the step, about to build): the **size cap**. When a
  target root's total exceeds 40 GiB, its units that are not in use are evicted largest
  first until the root is at most 40 GiB. If evicting every evictable unit could not bring
  the root to 40 GiB (in-use units and non-unit content alone exceed it), nothing is evicted
  and the step reports an unreachable cap (O3). That avoids a cold rebuild on every gate that
  cannot fix the overflow. A fresh warm root measures about 25 GiB (26 GB),
  so a normally warm root never trips it. The observed accumulation (123 GB in the main
  checkout) does. The idle rule does not apply here, because this checkout's gate is about
  to use its units.
* **Every other checkout:** the **idle rule**. A unit that is not in use is evicted when it
  has had no cargo write for 7 days, read from the newest mtime among the unit directory
  and those of its direct `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental` children that
  exist. An absent child is not an error. The size cap
  does not apply to other checkouts. Their own gates apply it. A fully fresh cargo run
  writes nothing in the unit (E2), so a checkout gated without compiling can look idle.
  The in-use test covers the one case where that matters, a test binary still running.

**Bound:** directly after the eviction step of a push gate with Rust work, the gating
checkout's target roots are each at most 40 GiB, unless in-use units or non-unit content
alone exceed it. Either case is reported as a warning (O3). Between gates a root grows by
whatever is built there. Nothing bounds that growth until the next gate, so it is not part
of the bound. The one-week `du -sh` observation therefore reads each gating checkout's
roots directly after an eviction step (its step log lists them), and reads every checkout
whose last compile is more than 7 days old after any later Rust push gate. The expected
values are at most 40 GiB and lock files only. Non-unit content in a root (for example `target/doc`) counts toward the
40 GiB and is reported, never removed. The constants sit beside the code with their
measurement basis, in the same style as the gate-runner constants. Unit tests pass
thresholds as function arguments. No runtime override exists.

### O2 — Eviction is safe against a live cargo

* **Lock held for the whole removal:** each unit is emptied in place by a remover that runs
  inside nested non-blocking `flock -n` holders on every Cargo lock file present in the unit
  (`.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock`, `.cargo-artifact-lock`). The locks therefore live
  exactly as long as the removal process and end with it on every exit path, including a
  killed parent. The holder processes are children of the step and are covered by the
  runner's existing child cleanup on signals.
* **Re-check under the lock:** the unit's rule, the in-use test (O1) and, for a mutation
  cache, the fence are evaluated again after the locks are acquired. If the unit no longer
  qualifies, nothing is removed.
* **What stays:** the lock files stay with their inodes, so a cargo that starts meanwhile
  blocks on the same inodes and then rebuilds into the emptied directory.
* **Order and stop rule:** `.fingerprint` is removed first and completely. If it cannot be
  removed completely, nothing else in the unit is touched and the unit fails (O3). Only
  then is every other entry removed, so any later partial state has no fingerprints and
  cargo rebuilds everything in it. An entry that cannot be removed in that second pass fails
  the unit, and the next unit continues.
* **Skips:** a held lock skips the unit. A `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` is skipped while
  `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress` exists. The fence and cache paths are imported
  from `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs`, which exports them as repository-relative
  constants, so the two cannot drift apart. Symlinked target roots, units or
  entries inside a unit are never followed. A symlink inside a unit is removed as a link,
  never through its target.
* **Fail direction is skip:** any undetermined observation skips the unit, never evicts, and
  counts as an undetermined skip (O3). That covers a `flock` exit other than 0 or 75, a
  failed `stat` or size walk, any fence-check error other than "absent", and a failure to
  list `/proc`, to stat `/proc/<pid>` or to read a same-user process's `exe` for a reason
  other than ENOENT, ESRCH or (for `exe`) EACCES. ENOENT and ESRCH mean the process exited.
  EACCES is the non-dumpable case in O1. When
  `git worktree list --porcelain` fails, only the own checkout is processed and the failure
  counts as undetermined. A listed path that is missing or prunable is skipped as expected.
  Every path comparison uses canonical paths on both sides.
* **Host:** a host without `flock` or `/proc` produces undetermined skips only, so nothing
  is evicted and the advisory warning shows (O3). No separate platform branch exists. The
  step is reachable only through `agent-gate`, which needs Linux systemd scopes.

### O3 — Advisory step: visible, never gating

* **Script outcome:** each unit's outcome is one line: evicted with bytes freed, skipped
  with an expected reason (in use, mutation fence, not idle, under the cap),
  skipped as undetermined with the observation error, or failed with error. An
  unreachable cap (O1 Bound) is one line too. After all units, one line per target root
  gives its total after eviction, which is what the one-week observation reads. The script catches every error at top level.
  It exits 0 when every outcome is evicted or an expected skip. It exits 1 when any unit
  failed, any skip was undetermined, the worktree listing failed or the cap was
  unreachable.
* **Runner:** the runner marks the eviction schedule entry advisory. An advisory step whose
  result is a non-zero exit, a spawn failure or a crash is recorded with status `warning`.
  `firstFailure`, the summary's failure trailer and lane admission ignore that status, so
  it never changes the run's exit code and never skips or blocks a later step or lane. The
  summary shows the warning row with its log tail. An interrupt of the whole run,
  and a child-termination failure that latches `childCleanup.error`, keep their existing
  handling. A result carrying a `terminationError` (unconfirmed child termination) is the
  exception to the `warning` mapping: it stays `failed` and latches `childCleanup.error` as
  today, because a lock holder may still be alive.

### O4 — Integration point

A new P0 step `rust-target-eviction` runs in `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` push mode, after
`mutation-guard` and before `setup-rust`, whenever the run selects the `rust` or `bindings`
block. Contract-only and frontend-only runs do not run it. Pre-review mode is unchanged: the
push gate of the same change runs the same Rust work afterwards, and pre-review repair loops
would otherwise re-walk every checkout. The runner currently addresses P0 entries by index
(`PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0[0]`, `p0[1]`), so the change makes every P0 consumer locate its
entry by name. It runs as a package script that `check-gate-routing.mjs` accepts. Every
checkout that pushes Rust changes runs this runner (drain, side lane, build worktree, main
checkout), so no new launcher, timer or service is needed.

### O5 — Ownership boundary with agent-kit

Removing a finished worktree is agent-kit's lifecycle (`kit worktree remove`, build Step 10)
and already deletes its `target/`. This project removes no worktree and touches no tracked
or untracked source file in any checkout. It empties only cargo build directories, which
are caches the next build regenerates. Idle eviction therefore bounds a retained worktree's
build output whatever happens to the worktree itself.

### O6 — Verification

Every assertion below must go red against a stub that removes nothing, ignores the lock or
evicts only the own checkout.

* **Eviction unit tests** (`node --test`, temporary fixtures):
  * Discovery uses a real `git init` plus `git worktree add`, run from one checkout. Stale
    units in the sibling and in the main checkout are evicted, and a missing or prunable
    listed path does not throw.
  * Idle rule: one case per signal (the unit directory and each of `deps`, `.fingerprint`,
    `build`, `incremental`) where only that one is fresh, and the unit is kept. A fully
    backdated unit is evicted. Cases sit just under and just over 7 days. A unit without
    `incremental`, and one without `build`, is evicted when backdated, kept when fresh, and
    exits 0.
  * In use: a live same-user process whose executable is a copy inside the unit keeps the
    unit under both rules. The same process with its executable outside the unit does not,
    nor does one inside a sibling directory whose name extends the unit's name. A checkout
    reached through a symlinked ancestor, with the executable running inside it, keeps its
    unit.
  * Size cap: allocated files push a root over a small threshold. Largest-first eviction
    stops once the root is under the threshold, and a hard-linked file is counted once.
    Non-unit content counts toward the total and is never removed. When non-unit content
    alone exceeds the threshold, nothing is evicted, the unreachable cap is reported and the
    exit is 1. The same holds when an in-use unit alone keeps the root over the threshold.
    The per-root total line is asserted after eviction. The own checkout is never idle-evicted. A unit over the
    threshold in another checkout, written recently, is kept.
  * Every skip case (each lock file, the mutation fence, in use, symlinked root or unit)
    uses a fixture that is otherwise evictable, and asserts both the reason line and the
    intact contents.
  * Locks: one skip case per lock file. A seam inside removal
    asserts that `flock -n -E 75` on each lock file returns 75 while removal runs. Lock
    files keep their inode numbers.
  * Order and stop rule: removal forced to stop part-way (an unremovable entry in the
    second pass) leaves `.fingerprint` gone and the remaining entries present. An
    unremovable entry inside `.fingerprint` leaves every other entry intact. Both fail
    the unit.
  * Re-check: through one seam between selection and lock acquisition, a unit refreshed
    there is kept, a unit that gains an in-use process there is kept, and a mutation cache
    whose fence appears there is kept.
  * Fail direction: an injected `flock` exit other than 0 or 75, a failed size walk, a
    fence-check error, a `/proc` read error other than ENOENT, ESRCH or EACCES, a missing
    `flock` binary, an absent `/proc`, EACCES on the stat of a same-user `/proc/<pid>`, and
    a failing worktree listing each keep the affected unit's contents intact and make the
    exit 1. Conversely, EACCES on a same-user `exe`, and ENOENT or ESRCH on `exe` or on the
    stat, leave an otherwise evictable unit evicted with exit 0.
  * Safety: the mutation fence skips the mutation cache. A symlink inside a unit pointing at
    an external directory is removed as a link, and the external files stay. Symlinked roots
    and units are not followed. An unremovable entry fails that unit, and the next unit is
    still evicted.
  * Constants: the shipped cap and idle constants are asserted.
* **Runner tests** (`scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`): the step runs after `mutation-guard`
  and before `setup-rust` with `--rust` and with `--bindings`, and is absent in
  contract-only and frontend-only runs. With the step exiting non-zero, failing to spawn and
  crashing, `setup-rust` and every selected lane still run, the exit code stays that of the
  other steps, and the summary shows the warning without the failure trailer. An interrupt
  during the step and a termination failure that latches `childCleanup.error` keep their
  existing outcomes. `setup-rust` and `mutation-guard` still
  resolve to their own commands. Pre-review command lists are unchanged.
* **Real-machine evidence:**
  * `du -sh` of every ChessFable target root and `mutants.out`, recorded before and after
    one real run of the eviction command, with its per-unit lines. At BASE, three
    side-lane worktrees exceed 7 idle days. Record which units were evicted and which
    were skipped, with the reasons.
  * Lock protection against a real cargo: a disposable probe crate whose `build.rs` sleeps
    builds under real cargo, while the exported eviction function runs against it with a
    threshold below its size. The recorded outcome is "skipped: in use (lock held)" and
    the build completes.
  * In-use protection against a real test binary: the same probe crate's sleeping test
    runs under `cargo test` while the function runs again. The recorded outcome is
    "skipped: in use (executable)" and the test passes.
  * Rebuild after eviction: the function then evicts the idle probe unit, and a following
    `cargo build` and `cargo test` of the probe crate succeed with the lock files' inodes
    unchanged.
* **Timing, in this build's own worktree:**
  * Wall time of a warm `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` before the change (baseline).
  * The same run with the step in place and nothing to evict (warm overhead).
  * One run after `target/debug` was emptied by hand, lock files kept (cold-with-sccache
    cost, the price of one cap eviction).
  * The growth of the root during that run, which is the O1 between-gates tolerance.
  * Pass criteria: with nothing to evict, the step's own runtime is at most 60 s, and the
    warm gate with the step is at most the baseline plus the step's runtime plus 5 %. A
    miss is a red proof, not a recorded number.
  * All times, that growth and the step's own runtime go into the closing note.
* **One-week observation:** the finding's one-week `du -sh` observation follows under
  next-finding §5 "Exit with an external wait" if it is still pending at release.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (full auto, technical):

* **Where eviction lives:** in the project push-gate runner (O4), not in a timer, not in the
  drain, not in pre-review and not in agent-kit. Rejected: a systemd timer or cron job (new
  machinery outside the repository, rule 6d); a drain step (misses side lanes and the main
  checkout, which never run a drain); pre-review wiring (repeats the walk on every repair
  round for work the push gate repeats anyway).
* **What it keeps:**
  * The gating checkout keeps at most 40 GiB per target root.
  * Other checkouts keep everything written within 7 days (O1).
  * No unit is evicted while it is in use: a lock is held or a same-user process runs an
    executable from it. Rejected: a working-directory check on `/proc/<pid>/cwd`. Felix's
    shells and editors sit in the main checkout permanently, which would exempt it forever.
    Other users' processes are unreadable, and a spelled path does not match the kernel's
    canonical one (round 2).
  * Rejected: cargo-sweep (a new tool whose time modes depend on access times, which the
    `noatime` mount never records); removing selected stale files inside `deps` (requires
    reproducing cargo's fingerprint graph); removing only `incremental` (leaves the stale
    `deps` accumulation the finding measured); a size cap on other checkouts (their own
    gates apply it, and the in-use test does not see a reader that only reads files, such
    as the coverage exporter after cargo exits);
    a per-unit cap (a root holds four units, so the per-root total would still reach
    about 128 GiB).
* **Shared target directory across worktrees: rejected.** The probe shows cargo holds all
  three build locks exclusively for the whole compile, so the drain, side lanes and build
  worktrees, which gate in parallel on two machine-wide gate slots, would serialize on one
  build directory. `run-backend-mutation.mjs` `prepareCache` also relies on each checkout's
  own target identity.
* **Worktree half:** belongs to agent-kit's worktree lifecycle, which already removes the
  whole tree (O5). No change there. The retained merged side-lane worktrees are not filed
  as a defect: they belong to Felix-authorized side-lane sessions, and nothing establishes
  they were meant to be removed. Idle eviction bounds their disk regardless.
* **Lock-held-in-place eviction instead of rename-then-delete:** renaming the unit away
  under a held lock lets a waiting cargo acquire the moved lock inode while a later cargo
  locks a fresh one at the original path. Emptying in place keeps one lock inode per path.

## Risks / open questions

* A gate that starts on an oversize root pays one cold build with sccache. Measured in O6.
  The trip rate is not known in advance. The finding's 6.9 to 14.1 GB per day were the
  first days of a fresh worktree, which include its initial builds. The drain worktree now
  holds 27 GB four days after it was recreated, against about 26 GB for a fresh warm root,
  so its steady growth there was about a quarter GB per day. At that rate the 15 GiB of
  headroom lasts weeks, while the main checkout's months-long 123 GB shows it is eventually
  needed. The one-week observation records the actual rate. A trip more often than weekly
  is a reason to revisit the cap.
* Cargo may change its lock file names. A unit is recognised by `.cargo-lock`, and every
  lock file present is held, so an added lock file name is the only gap. Stated as a known
  limit in the code comment.
* A process that reads a unit's files without running an executable from it (for example
  the coverage exporter reading `llvm-cov-target` after cargo exits) is not seen by the
  in-use test. In ChessFable's gates such a reader runs right after `cargo llvm-cov` ran
  test binaries from the same unit, so an eviction would have to land in the seconds
  between the last test binary and the export. Under the idle rule that also needs 7 days
  without a compile in that checkout (E2 allows it). Under the own-checkout cap it needs a
  second concurrent gate in the same checkout. Known limit.
* Between two test binaries of one `cargo test`, for milliseconds, neither lock nor
  executable is present. Known limit, only reachable by a concurrent foreign `cargo test`
  on an own-checkout root over the cap, or on a unit idle for 7 days.
* A unit skipped on every run (a lock held indefinitely, or a stale mutation fence left by
  a crashed runner in a checkout that never gates) is reported in each step log and never
  evicted. The gating checkout's own `mutation-guard` already refuses a stale fence there.
  Known limit.
* `/tmp` target copies made ad hoc by earlier sessions are outside every repository root
  and out of scope.

## Not part of this task

* Removing or pruning worktrees (agent-kit lifecycle, O5).
* sccache's own cache size (sccache enforces its configured maximum itself).
* CI caches, cargo registry and git caches under `~/.cargo`.
* Pre-review mode, and any change to gate content, receipts or coverage. The only gate-order
  change is the one advisory P0 step.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — eviction step, wiring, tests, rule text

* **Files:**
  * New `scripts/rust-target-eviction.mjs` and `scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs`.
  * `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs`: the advisory P0 step, advisory result handling, P0 lookup
    by name.
  * `scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`.
  * `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`: its pinned `CONTRACT_CHAIN` gains the new test
    script.
  * `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs`: export its existing repository-relative `fencePath`
    and a repository-relative cache-path constant that `prepareCache` then uses, for the
    eviction step. No behaviour change.
  * `package.json`: `rust:target:evict` and `rust:target:evict:test`, the latter chained
    into `gates:contract:check`.
  * `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs`, only if its route table needs the new command.
  * `.claude/rules/gate-tooling.md`: correct the "mutation guard first (always) and
    `setup-rust`" sentence to name the eviction step. Add one short "Rust build output
    eviction" section stating O1–O3 and naming the decision id the adopter records for this
    plan.
* **Surfaces:** filesystem deletion inside other checkouts, concurrency with live cargo
  processes, and `package.json` (sensitive glob). Role `sensitive`.
* **PROOF COMMAND:** `node --test scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs && pnpm gates:push:test && pnpm gates:routing:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm mutation:runner:test && pnpm checks:pre-review`, then the O6 real-machine evidence and timing runs, recorded with their outputs and wall times.

## Carried to diff review

* CR-1 — The failure-isolation test (unremovable entry) skips itself when run as root. The
  timing runs report each measurement's sample count. (from I15)
* CR-2 — The remover never exits 75, so its failures cannot be read as "lock held" by the
  `flock -n -E 75` wrapper. The remover reports partial removal through its own channel, so
  the log never says "contents intact" after a partial removal. A `/proc/<pid>/exe` target
  ending in " (deleted)" is compared without that suffix, with a test. (from I27 and
  round-3 nits)
* CR-4 — Round-4 test anchors: a both-hold case shows the lock reason; the exported fence
  and cache constants are pinned, and `prepareCache` uses the exported cache constant; the
  re-check seam also sits after lock acquisition, inside the remover; the re-check's in-use
  test ignores the locks the remover itself holds. (from I68)
* CR-3 — Fixture and walker details from round 3: bigint `lstat` identities for the inode
  dedupe; `git` fixtures pass `-c user.name -c user.email`; the Linux-only fixtures skip
  when `flock` or `/proc` are absent or `os.tmpdir()` is `noexec`; the lock holders keep the
  lock fd across the remover (util-linux `flock` passes it to the command by default),
  so killing a holder alone cannot release the lock while removal continues.

### plan-r6.md

# Plan: bound Rust build output in every ChessFable checkout (f-20261017-04)

## Goal

Rust build output in ChessFable checkouts gets a ceiling without manual cleanup. Directly
after each Rust push gate's eviction step, the gating checkout's target roots are at most a
stated cap, or the step warns that the cap could not be reached. A checkout nobody compiles
in for 7 days loses its evictable build units at the next Rust push gate anywhere, and any
unit kept for an undetermined reason makes the step warn. Gates in the checkout that is
building stay warm in the ordinary case.

## MANDATE

Verbatim from `tasks/findings.md`, f-20261017-04:

* **Where:** `src-tauri/target/` in the main checkout and in every worktree that runs the Rust gates (`chessfable.worktrees/drain/src-tauri/target/`), plus `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target/`. Written by the gate scripts that drive cargo: `scripts/gate-receipt.mjs`, `scripts/rust-branch-coverage.mjs` / `scripts/coverage-report.mjs` (`llvm-cov-target`), `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs` (`mutants.out`). No script, gate or drain step ever removes stale artifacts.
* **Defect:** cargo keeps every artifact for every dependency version, feature set and profile it has ever built, and nothing in the project evicts them. Measured on tuxedo-atlas 2026-10-17: main checkout `src-tauri/target` 123 GB (`debug/incremental` 56 GB across 86 crate-session directories, `debug/deps` 22 GB of which 3,640 files date from August and 2,743 from September, `llvm-cov-target` 23 GB, cross targets 13 GB). `chessfable.worktrees/drain/src-tauri/target` 32 GB, all written since 2026-10-14 (17.3 GB on 10-14, 6.9 GB on 10-16, 14.1 GB on 10-17). `mutants.out` 6.9 GB, plus an 8 GB copy of a target under `/tmp/catalog-engine-phase1-20261016/snapshot`. The main target wrote 12.1 GB on 10-11 and 9.1 GB on 10-13. Together that is about 160 GB on a root filesystem that ran full on 2026-10-12 (from a different cause, agent-kit `f-20261012-10`). It has no ceiling. sccache (`~/.cache/sccache`, 5.6 GB) is already present, so a cold rebuild after eviction costs less than a cold build without it.
* **Open question:** where eviction lives and what it keeps. Options include: delete a worktree's `target/` when its build/drain run lands; evict artifacts not used for N days in long-lived targets (main checkout, the reused `drain` worktree) from a gate wrapper; cap total size; or share one target directory per profile across worktrees. The plan must weigh warm-cache gate times (measured before and after) against the bound, and decide whether the worktree half belongs here or in agent-kit's worktree lifecycle.
* **Proof:** after one week of normal drain activity with the fix in place, `du -sh` of every ChessFable `target/` and `mutants.out` stays under the chosen bound, and warm gate timings stay within the measured baseline.

## Threat model and non-goals

Inputs are this repository's own checkouts on Felix's Linux workstation. The adversary is
concurrency, not malice: another session may be running cargo in any checkout (a drain, a
side lane, a build worktree, a leaf, Felix by hand) at the moment eviction runs, and a
previous eviction may have been interrupted. Symlinked or foreign directories must never be
followed. CI runners are ephemeral and out of scope. Non-Linux hosts are out of scope beyond
not failing a gate. Deliberately evasive layouts are out of scope.

## Traced premises

Measured 2026-10-31 at BASE 385287ef:

* Current sizes: main checkout `src-tauri/target` 6.4 GB (cleaned by hand at some point),
  `chessfable.worktrees/drain` 27 GB (every entry dated 2026-10-27 or later, so it was also
  recreated by hand), `side-lane-codex-20261027` 26 GB, `side-lane-codex-20261023` 9.7 GB,
  `side-lane-astra-20261023` 8.8 GB, `side-lane-codex-20261022` 2.0 GB. No `mutants.out` in
  the main checkout today. `/` is 42 % used.
* A freshly built warm checkout weighs about 26 GB: `debug` 17 GB (`incremental` 7.5,
  `deps` 6.8, `build` 2.5), `llvm-cov-target` 6.3 GB, `release` 2.0 GB,
  `x86_64-pc-windows-gnu` 1.4 GB (drain and side-lane-codex-20261027 measured identically).
* All four side-lane branches are fully merged into master and their worktrees are clean
  (`git rev-list --count master..build/<branch>` = 0, `git status --porcelain` empty). They
  were never removed, so their build output (46.5 GB) idles. `kit worktree remove`
  (agent-kit `lib/kit_worktree.py`, `remove`) already deletes a whole linked worktree,
  `target/` included, once its branch is merged and the tree is clean.
* `/` is mounted `noatime` (`mount`), so access-time based eviction (cargo-sweep `--time`
  and `--stamp`/`--file` modes) cannot tell a used artifact from an unused one. Modification
  times are reliable. Every compile creates a new `incremental/<crate>-<hash>/s-*` session and
  rewrites the `deps` directory entries of the workspace crate, which updates those
  directories' mtimes (probe: `deps` mtime moved to the build's end time).
* Cargo 1.98.0 creates `.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock` and `.cargo-artifact-lock` in each
  build directory (`target/debug`, `target/release`,
  `target/x86_64-pc-windows-gnu/debug`, `target/llvm-cov-target/debug`). Disposable probe
  under `$RUN_TMP/cargo-lock-probe` (a crate whose `build.rs` sleeps 25 s): during the build
  `flock -n -E 75 <file> true` exited 75 for all three files. After the build all three
  exited 0. `flock` is `/usr/bin/flock` (util-linux).
* sccache serves every local compile through `rustc-wrapper` in `~/.cargo/config.toml`.
  Every `kit worktree create` checkout already starts with an empty `target/`, so a cold
  build with sccache is the normal first gate in every build worktree today.
* `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` `PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0` runs `mutation-guard` (always) and
  then `setup-rust` (blocks `rust`, `bindings`) before any cargo lane. The pre-review mode
  runs `pnpm rust:windows:check` among its lanes. `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs` validates
  every schedule command against package scripts. `gates:contract:check` chains every
  `node --test scripts/*-tests.mjs` package script.
* `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs` `prepareCache` keeps cargo output in
  `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` and holds the
  `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress` fence while a mutation run is live.
* Receipts in `.gate-receipts/*.json` carry no durations. No eviction code exists:
  `git grep -n -E "cargo[- ]sweep|evict"` in `scripts/`, `.github/`, `.claude/skills/` finds none.

## Approach

### O1 — Unit, roots, in-use signal and the two eviction rules

The eviction unit is one cargo build directory: a directory that directly contains
`.cargo-lock`, found at most three levels below a cargo target root. The target roots are
`src-tauri/target` and `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` of every checkout of this
repository, as listed by `git worktree list --porcelain` (the main checkout included).
Size is allocated blocks of every entry (files, directories and symlinks, by `lstat`), each
inode counted once by device and inode number. That is the measure `du` reports, so
hard-linked incremental and uplifted artifacts are not counted twice and the bound is checked
with the same measure as the finding's `du -sh`.

**Evictable:** a unit is evictable when its lock test and in-use test (below) are negative,
it is not a fenced mutation cache, and no observation about it was undetermined (O2).

**In use:** a unit is in use when any of its Cargo lock files is held, or when a live
process of the current user runs an executable located inside the unit. The lock test runs
first, and its result is the reported reason when both hold. The second test compares
`/proc/<pid>/exe` with the unit's canonical path, path segment by path segment. Cargo frees
its build locks while test binaries run (Reviews, evidence E1), and a running test binary is
exactly a process whose executable lies in the unit. A process counts as the current user's
when the owner uid of `/proc/<pid>` equals the step's uid. Other users' processes are not
inspected. A same-user process whose `exe` link is unreadable (EACCES) is ignored: on this
host those are non-dumpable desktop daemons (E5). The one cargo-built binary in this
repository that makes itself non-dumpable is the encoding mutation test
(`src-tauri/src/db/encoding.rs`, `prctl(PR_SET_DUMPABLE, 0)`), and only under the mutation
runner. That case is protected by the mutation fence (O2), not by this test. An in-use unit
is never evicted, under either rule.

Two rules, split by which checkout a unit belongs to:

* **Own checkout** (the one running the step, about to build): the **size cap**. When a
  target root's total exceeds 40 GiB, its units that are not in use are evicted largest
  first until the root is at most 40 GiB. If evicting every evictable unit could not bring
  the root to 40 GiB (the units that are not evictable and non-unit content alone exceed
  it), nothing is evicted and the step reports an unreachable cap (O3). That avoids a cold
  rebuild on every gate that cannot fix the overflow. A unit that fails its re-check under
  the lock is skipped, and selection continues with the next-largest unit. After the loop
  the root's actual total is compared with the cap. A root still above 40 GiB reports the
  unreachable cap too. A fresh warm root measures about 25 GiB (26 GB),
  so a normally warm root never trips it. The observed accumulation (123 GB in the main
  checkout) does. The idle rule does not apply here, because this checkout's gate is about
  to use its units.
* **Every other checkout:** the **idle rule**. A unit that is not in use is evicted when it
  has had no cargo write for 7 days, read from the newest mtime among the unit directory
  and those of its direct `deps`, `.fingerprint`, `build` and `incremental` children that
  exist. An absent child is not an error. The size cap
  does not apply to other checkouts. Their own gates apply it. A fully fresh cargo run
  writes nothing in the unit (E2), so a checkout gated without compiling can look idle.
  The in-use test covers the one case where that matters, a test binary still running.

**Bound:** directly after the eviction step of a push gate with Rust work, the gating
checkout's target roots are each at most 40 GiB, unless units that are not evictable or
non-unit content keep a root above it. Either case is reported as a warning (O3). Between gates a root grows by
whatever is built there. Nothing bounds that growth until the next gate, so it is not part
of the bound. The one-week `du -sh` observation therefore reads each gating checkout's
roots directly after an eviction step (its step log lists them), and reads every checkout
whose last compile is more than 7 days old after any later Rust push gate. The expected
values are at most 40 GiB, and lock files plus non-unit content (for example
`.rustc_info.json`, `CACHEDIR.TAG`) only. Non-unit content in a root (for example `target/doc`) counts toward the
40 GiB and is reported, never removed. The constants sit beside the code with their
measurement basis, in the same style as the gate-runner constants. Unit tests pass
thresholds as function arguments. No runtime override exists.

### O2 — Eviction is safe against a live cargo

* **Lock held for the whole removal:** each unit is emptied in place by a remover that runs
  inside nested non-blocking `flock -n` holders on every Cargo lock file present in the unit
  (`.cargo-lock`, `.cargo-build-lock`, `.cargo-artifact-lock`). The locks therefore live
  exactly as long as the removal process and end with it on every exit path, including a
  killed parent. The holder processes are children of the step and are covered by the
  runner's existing child cleanup on signals.
* **Re-check under the lock:** the unit's rule, the in-use test (O1) and, for a mutation
  cache, the fence are evaluated again after the locks are acquired. If the unit no longer
  qualifies, nothing is removed.
* **What stays:** the lock files stay with their inodes, so a cargo that starts meanwhile
  blocks on the same inodes and then rebuilds into the emptied directory.
* **Order and stop rule:** `.fingerprint` is removed first and completely. If it cannot be
  removed completely, nothing else in the unit is touched and the unit fails (O3). Only
  then is every other entry removed, so any later partial state has no fingerprints and
  cargo rebuilds everything in it. An entry that cannot be removed in that second pass fails
  the unit, and the next unit continues.
* **Skips:** a held lock skips the unit. A `mutants.out/backend/cargo-target` is skipped while
  `mutants.out/backend/.mutation-in-progress` exists. The fence and cache paths are imported
  from `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs`, which exports them as repository-relative
  constants, so the two cannot drift apart. Symlinked target roots, units or
  entries inside a unit are never followed. A symlink inside a unit is removed as a link,
  never through its target.
* **Fail direction is skip:** any undetermined observation skips the unit, never evicts, and
  counts as an undetermined skip (O3). That covers a `flock` exit other than 0 or 75, a
  failed `stat` or size walk, any fence-check error other than "absent", and a failure to
  list `/proc`, to stat `/proc/<pid>` or to read a same-user process's `exe` for a reason
  other than ENOENT, ESRCH or (for `exe`) EACCES. ENOENT and ESRCH mean the process exited.
  EACCES is the non-dumpable case in O1. When
  `git worktree list --porcelain` fails, only the own checkout is processed and the failure
  counts as undetermined. A listed path that is missing or prunable is skipped as expected.
  Every path comparison uses canonical paths on both sides.
* **Host:** a host without `flock` or `/proc` produces undetermined skips only, so nothing
  is evicted and the advisory warning shows (O3). No separate platform branch exists. The
  step is reachable only through `agent-gate`, which needs Linux systemd scopes.

### O3 — Advisory step: visible, never gating

* **Script outcome:** each unit's outcome is one line: evicted with bytes freed, skipped
  with an expected reason (in use, mutation fence, not idle, under the cap),
  skipped as undetermined with the observation error, or failed with error. An
  unreachable cap (O1 Bound) is one line too. After all units, one line per target root
  gives its total after eviction, which is what the one-week observation reads. The script catches every error at top level.
  It exits 0 when every outcome is evicted or an expected skip. It exits 1 when any unit
  failed, any skip was undetermined, the worktree listing failed or the cap was
  unreachable.
* **Runner:** the runner marks the eviction schedule entry advisory. An advisory step whose
  result is a non-zero exit, a spawn failure or a crash is recorded with status `warning`.
  `firstFailure`, the summary's failure trailer and lane admission ignore that status, so
  it never changes the run's exit code and never skips or blocks a later step or lane. The
  summary shows the warning row with its log tail. An interrupt of the whole run,
  and a child-termination failure that latches `childCleanup.error`, keep their existing
  handling. A result carrying a `terminationError` (unconfirmed child termination) is the
  exception to the `warning` mapping: it stays `failed` and latches `childCleanup.error` as
  today, because a lock holder may still be alive.

### O4 — Integration point

A new P0 step `rust-target-eviction` runs in `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs` push mode, after
`mutation-guard` and before `setup-rust`, whenever the run selects the `rust` or `bindings`
block. Contract-only and frontend-only runs do not run it. Pre-review mode is unchanged: the
push gate of the same change runs the same Rust work afterwards, and pre-review repair loops
would otherwise re-walk every checkout. The runner currently addresses P0 entries by index
(`PUSH_GATE_SCHEDULE.p0[0]`, `p0[1]`), so the change makes every P0 consumer locate its
entry by name. It runs as a package script that `check-gate-routing.mjs` accepts. Every
checkout that pushes Rust changes runs this runner (drain, side lane, build worktree, main
checkout), so no new launcher, timer or service is needed.

### O5 — Ownership boundary with agent-kit

Removing a finished worktree is agent-kit's lifecycle (`kit worktree remove`, build Step 10)
and already deletes its `target/`. This project removes no worktree and touches no tracked
or untracked source file in any checkout. It empties only cargo build directories, which
are caches the next build regenerates. Idle eviction therefore bounds a retained worktree's
build output whatever happens to the worktree itself.

### O6 — Verification

Every assertion below must go red against a stub that removes nothing, ignores the lock or
evicts only the own checkout.

* **Eviction unit tests** (`node --test`, temporary fixtures):
  * Discovery uses a real `git init` plus `git worktree add`, run from one checkout. Stale
    units in the sibling and in the main checkout are evicted, and a missing or prunable
    listed path does not throw.
  * Idle rule: one case per signal (the unit directory and each of `deps`, `.fingerprint`,
    `build`, `incremental`) where only that one is fresh, and the unit is kept. A fully
    backdated unit is evicted. Cases sit just under and just over 7 days. A unit without
    `incremental`, and one without `build`, is evicted when backdated, kept when fresh, and
    exits 0.
  * In use: a live same-user process whose executable is a copy inside the unit keeps the
    unit under both rules. The same process with its executable outside the unit does not,
    nor does one inside a sibling directory whose name extends the unit's name. A checkout
    reached through a symlinked ancestor, with the executable running inside it, keeps its
    unit.
  * Size cap: allocated files push a root over a small threshold. Largest-first eviction
    stops once the root is under the threshold, and a hard-linked file is counted once.
    Non-unit content counts toward the total and is never removed. When non-unit content
    alone exceeds the threshold, nothing is evicted, the unreachable cap is reported and the
    exit is 1. The same holds when an in-use unit alone keeps the root over the threshold.
    The per-root total line is asserted after eviction. A unit that turns in-use at the
    re-check is skipped, the next-largest unit is evicted instead, and a root left over the
    threshold reports the unreachable cap with exit 1. A sparse file with a large apparent
    size and few allocated blocks does not trip the threshold. The own checkout is never idle-evicted. A unit over the
    threshold in another checkout, written recently, is kept.
  * Every skip case (each lock file, the mutation fence, in use, symlinked root or unit)
    uses a fixture that is otherwise evictable, and asserts both the reason line and the
    intact contents.
  * Locks: one skip case per lock file. A seam inside removal
    asserts that `flock -n -E 75` on each lock file returns 75 while removal runs. Lock
    files keep their inode numbers.
  * Order and stop rule: removal forced to stop part-way (an unremovable entry in the
    second pass) leaves `.fingerprint` gone and the remaining entries present. An
    unremovable entry inside `.fingerprint` leaves every other entry intact. Both fail
    the unit.
  * Re-check: through one seam between selection and lock acquisition, a unit refreshed
    there is kept, a unit that gains an in-use process there is kept, and a mutation cache
    whose fence appears there is kept.
  * Fail direction: an injected `flock` exit other than 0 or 75, a failed size walk, a
    fence-check error, a `/proc` read error other than ENOENT, ESRCH or EACCES, a missing
    `flock` binary, an absent `/proc`, EACCES on the stat of a same-user `/proc/<pid>`, and
    a failing worktree listing each keep the affected unit's contents intact and make the
    exit 1. Conversely, EACCES on a same-user `exe`, and ENOENT or ESRCH on `exe` or on the
    stat, leave an otherwise evictable unit evicted with exit 0.
  * Safety: the mutation fence skips the mutation cache. A symlink inside a unit pointing at
    an external directory is removed as a link, and the external files stay. Symlinked roots
    and units are not followed. An unremovable entry fails that unit, and the next unit is
    still evicted.
  * Constants: the shipped cap and idle constants are asserted.
* **Runner tests** (`scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`): the step runs after `mutation-guard`
  and before `setup-rust` with `--rust` and with `--bindings`, and is absent in
  contract-only and frontend-only runs. With the step exiting non-zero, failing to spawn and
  crashing, `setup-rust` and every selected lane still run, the exit code stays that of the
  other steps, and the summary shows the warning without the failure trailer. An interrupt
  during the step keeps its existing outcome. An advisory step whose supervisor reports
  unconfirmed termination is recorded `failed`, not `warning`, makes the run exit non-zero,
  sets `childCleanup.error` and refuses later starts. `setup-rust` and `mutation-guard` still
  resolve to their own commands. Pre-review command lists are unchanged.
* **Real-machine evidence:**
  * `du -sh` of every ChessFable target root and `mutants.out`, recorded before and after
    one real run of the eviction command, with its per-unit lines. The three side-lane
    worktrees created on 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-23 are expected to be past 7 idle days,
    which the run confirms or refutes. Record which units were evicted and which were
    skipped, with the reasons.
  * Lock protection against a real cargo: a disposable probe crate whose `build.rs` sleeps
    builds under real cargo, while the exported eviction function runs against it with a
    threshold below its size. The recorded outcome is "skipped: in use (lock held)" and
    the build completes.
  * In-use protection against a real test binary: the same probe crate's sleeping test
    runs under `cargo test` while the function runs again. The recorded outcome is
    "skipped: in use (executable)" and the test passes.
  * Rebuild after eviction: the function then evicts the idle probe unit, and a following
    `cargo build` and `cargo test` of the probe crate succeed with the lock files' inodes
    unchanged.
* **Timing, in this build's own worktree:**
  * Wall time of a warm `pnpm gates:push -- --rust` before the change (baseline).
  * The same run with the step in place and nothing to evict (warm overhead).
  * One run after `target/debug` was emptied by hand, lock files kept (cold-with-sccache
    cost, the price of one cap eviction).
  * The growth of the root during that run, which is the O1 between-gates tolerance.
  * Pass criteria: with nothing to evict, the step's own runtime is at most 60 s, and the
    warm gate with the step is at most the baseline plus the step's runtime plus 5 %. A
    miss is a red proof, not a recorded number.
  * All times, that growth and the step's own runtime go into the closing note.
* **One-week observation:** the finding's one-week `du -sh` observation follows under
  next-finding §5 "Exit with an external wait" if it is still pending at release.

## Decisions and trade-offs

Decided autonomously (full auto, technical):

* **Where eviction lives:** in the project push-gate runner (O4), not in a timer, not in the
  drain, not in pre-review and not in agent-kit. Rejected: a systemd timer or cron job (new
  machinery outside the repository, rule 6d); a drain step (misses side lanes and the main
  checkout, which never run a drain); pre-review wiring (repeats the walk on every repair
  round for work the push gate repeats anyway).
* **What it keeps:**
  * The gating checkout keeps at most 40 GiB per target root.
  * Other checkouts keep everything written within 7 days (O1).
  * No unit is evicted while it is in use: a lock is held or a same-user process runs an
    executable from it. Rejected: a working-directory check on `/proc/<pid>/cwd`. Felix's
    shells and editors sit in the main checkout permanently, which would exempt it forever.
    Other users' processes are unreadable, and a spelled path does not match the kernel's
    canonical one (round 2).
  * Rejected: cargo-sweep (a new tool whose time modes depend on access times, which the
    `noatime` mount never records); removing selected stale files inside `deps` (requires
    reproducing cargo's fingerprint graph); removing only `incremental` (leaves the stale
    `deps` accumulation the finding measured); a size cap on other checkouts (their own
    gates apply it, and the in-use test does not see a reader that only reads files, such
    as the coverage exporter after cargo exits);
    a per-unit cap (a root holds four units, so the per-root total would still reach
    about 128 GiB).
* **Shared target directory across worktrees: rejected.** The probe shows cargo holds all
  three build locks exclusively for the whole compile, so the drain, side lanes and build
  worktrees, which gate in parallel on two machine-wide gate slots, would serialize on one
  build directory. `run-backend-mutation.mjs` `prepareCache` also relies on each checkout's
  own target identity.
* **Worktree half:** belongs to agent-kit's worktree lifecycle, which already removes the
  whole tree (O5). No change there. The retained merged side-lane worktrees are not filed
  as a defect: they belong to Felix-authorized side-lane sessions, and nothing establishes
  they were meant to be removed. Idle eviction bounds their disk regardless.
* **Lock-held-in-place eviction instead of rename-then-delete:** renaming the unit away
  under a held lock lets a waiting cargo acquire the moved lock inode while a later cargo
  locks a fresh one at the original path. Emptying in place keeps one lock inode per path.

## Risks / open questions

* A gate that starts on an oversize root pays one cold build with sccache. Measured in O6.
  The trip rate is not known in advance, because growth follows the number of compiles
  rather than time. The one-week observation records the actual rate. A trip more often
  than weekly is a reason to revisit the cap.
* Cargo may change its lock file names. A unit is recognised by `.cargo-lock`, and every
  lock file present is held, so an added lock file name is the only gap. Stated as a known
  limit in the code comment.
* A process that reads a unit's files without running an executable from it (for example
  the coverage exporter reading `llvm-cov-target` after cargo exits) is not seen by the
  in-use test. In ChessFable's gates such a reader runs right after `cargo llvm-cov` ran
  test binaries from the same unit, so an eviction would have to land in the seconds
  between the last test binary and the export. Under the idle rule that also needs 7 days
  without a compile in that checkout (E2 allows it). Under the own-checkout cap it needs a
  second concurrent gate in the same checkout. Known limit.
* Between two test binaries of one `cargo test`, for milliseconds, neither lock nor
  executable is present. Known limit, only reachable by a concurrent foreign `cargo test`
  on an own-checkout root over the cap, or on a unit idle for 7 days.
* A unit skipped on every run (a lock held indefinitely, or a stale mutation fence left by
  a crashed runner in a checkout that never gates) is reported in each step log and never
  evicted. The gating checkout's own `mutation-guard` already refuses a stale fence there.
  Known limit.
* `/tmp` target copies made ad hoc by earlier sessions are outside every repository root
  and out of scope.

## Not part of this task

* Removing or pruning worktrees (agent-kit lifecycle, O5).
* sccache's own cache size (sccache enforces its configured maximum itself).
* CI caches, cargo registry and git caches under `~/.cargo`.
* Pre-review mode, and any change to gate content, receipts or coverage. The only gate-order
  change is the one advisory P0 step.

## Phases

### Phase 1 — eviction step, wiring, tests, rule text

* **Files:**
  * New `scripts/rust-target-eviction.mjs` and `scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs`.
  * `scripts/run-push-gates.mjs`: the advisory P0 step, advisory result handling, P0 lookup
    by name.
  * `scripts/run-push-gates-tests.mjs`.
  * `scripts/check-gate-routing-tests.mjs`: its pinned `CONTRACT_CHAIN` gains the new test
    script.
  * `scripts/run-backend-mutation.mjs`: export its existing repository-relative `fencePath`
    and a repository-relative cache-path constant that `prepareCache` then uses, for the
    eviction step. No behaviour change.
  * `package.json`: `rust:target:evict` and `rust:target:evict:test`, the latter chained
    into `gates:contract:check`.
  * `scripts/check-gate-routing.mjs`, only if its route table needs the new command.
  * `.claude/rules/gate-tooling.md`: correct the "mutation guard first (always) and
    `setup-rust`" sentence to name the eviction step. Add one short "Rust build output
    eviction" section stating O1–O3 and naming the decision id the adopter records for this
    plan.
* **Surfaces:** filesystem deletion inside other checkouts, concurrency with live cargo
  processes, and `package.json` (sensitive glob). Role `sensitive`.
* **PROOF COMMAND:** `node --test scripts/rust-target-eviction-tests.mjs && pnpm gates:push:test && pnpm gates:routing:check && pnpm gates:routing:test && pnpm mutation:runner:test && pnpm checks:pre-review`, then the O6 real-machine evidence and timing runs, recorded with their outputs and wall times.

## Carried to diff review

* CR-1 — The failure-isolation test (unremovable entry) skips itself when run as root. The
  timing runs report each measurement's sample count. (from I15)
* CR-2 — The remover never exits 75, so its failures cannot be read as "lock held" by the
  `flock -n -E 75` wrapper. The remover reports partial removal through its own channel, so
  the log never says "contents intact" after a partial removal. A `/proc/<pid>/exe` target
  ending in " (deleted)" is compared without that suffix, with a test. (from I27 and
  round-3 nits)
* CR-4 — Round-4 test anchors: a both-hold case shows the lock reason; the exported fence
  and cache constants are pinned, and `prepareCache` uses the exported cache constant; the
  re-check seam also sits after lock acquisition, inside the remover; the re-check's in-use
  test ignores the locks the remover itself holds. (from I68)
* CR-3 — Fixture and walker details from round 3: bigint `lstat` identities for the inode
  dedupe; `git` fixtures pass `-c user.name -c user.email`; the Linux-only fixtures skip
  when `flock` or `/proc` are absent or `os.tmpdir()` is `noexec`; the lock holders keep the
  lock fd across the remover (util-linux `flock` passes it to the command by default),
  so killing a holder alone cannot release the lock while removal continues.

### Round 5 (revision r5, 2026-10-31, wall 202 s for the lens group)

**I39 lineage judgment (second failed closure, build §4 escalation):** the invariant is
that Risks must not claim a trip rate the evidence does not support. The previous answer
derived a rate from the finding's daily figures, then from two rounded sizes of different

than weekly reopens the cap. Changed: the Risks bullet. The focused fresh-context review-plan
check of this judgment runs in round 6.
